- **Fix:** Add an adrenaline panel to Auga's HUD prefab (clone the StaminaBar AugaHealthBar setup, add an AugaHealthBar.ModeType.Adrenaline mode reading Player.GetAdrenaline()/GetMaxAdrenaline()), CopyOver it in Hud_Awake_Postfix beside `hudroot/StaminaBar`, add a `[HarmonyPatch(nameof(Hud.UpdateAdrenaline))] [HarmonyPrefix] return false` next to the existing UpdateStamina/UpdateEitr prefixes, null out m_adrenalineBarRoot/Fast/Slow/Text, repoint m_adrenalineAnimator at the new panel (AdrenalineBarFlash needs it non-null), and register a MovableHudElement for it. Minimum viable stopgap: `Object.Destroy(__instance.transform.Find("hudroot/adrenalinepanel").gameObject)` plus the return-false prefix, which at least stops the un-styled bar appearing.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix is sound. Worth noting Auga's own PORT_1.0.md:54 already lists the adrenaline bar as a known unbuilt prefab, so this is a tracked gap rather than a surprise.

### [auga-lib-1] BuildMenuPaginationController casts the category-tab array index to Piece.PieceCategory, which 1.0 renumbered and decoupled from the tab order

- **Verdict:** partly (reviewer confidence 92)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/BuildMenuPaginationController.cs:107-123 (RefreshVisibleObjects) and :63 + :129-134 (ToggleBuildMenuTabs)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud.UpdateBuild (decomp/Hud.cs:1462-1481), Piece.PieceCategory (decomp/Piece.cs), PieceTable.GetSelectedCategory / GetAvailablePiecesInCategory (decomp/PieceTable.cs:148-170)
- **Trigger:** Equip a hammer/hoe/cultivator/cauldron piece table and open the build menu; every frame the menu is open.
- **Consequence:** Tab visibility and the '[n]' available-piece counts are computed for the wrong category for any piece table whose m_categories list is not literally [0,1,2,...]. For the cooking/feast tables (categories Food=7, Meads=8, Feasts=6) the counts come from Misc/Crafting/BuildingWorkbench instead, so real tabs get hidden by `if (buildPieces == 0) category.SetActive(false)`. When m_selectedCategory is one of the new values (DeepNorth=5, Feasts=6, Food=7, Meads=8) or still the lazy default Max=9, `_visibleObjects.FirstOrDefault(x => x.Value.Key.Equals(categoryId))` returns default(KeyValuePair) and `visibleObject.Value.Value.transform` throws an NRE that the `catch (Exception) {}` in Update swallows — the category title and the page arrows then freeze on the last good value.
- **Evidence:** Auga: `var buildPieces = _player.m_buildPieces.GetAvailablePiecesInCategory((Piece.PieceCategory)i);` (line 110) and `currentStartIndex = (int)_player.m_buildPieces.m_selectedCategory;` (line 63). 0.217 Hud did the same: `m_buildCategoryNames[i] + " [<color=yellow>" + player.GetAvailableBuildPiecesInCategory((Piece.PieceCategory)i)` (decomp_old_assembly_valheim/Hud.cs:1250). 1.0 Hud instead does `bool flag = i < pieceTable.m_categories.Count; ... player.GetAvailableBuildPiecesInCategory(pieceTable.m_categories[i])` (decomp/Hud.cs:1467-1471). Enum renumbered: OLD `Building = 2, Furniture = 3, Max = 4`; NEW `BuildingWorkbench = 2, BuildingStonecutter = 3, Furniture = 4, DeepNorth = 5, Feasts = 6, Food = 7, Meads = 8, Max = 9` (auga_audit/diffs/Piece.diff). 1.0 also made the field private with a lazy default: `private Piece.PieceCategory m_selectedCategory = Piece.PieceCategory.Max;` and `GetSelectedCategory()` only resolves it to `m_categories[0]` on first call (decomp/PieceTable.cs:35,148-158). OLD was `public Piece.PieceCategory m_selectedCategory;` (decomp_old_assembly_valheim/PieceTable.cs:21).
- **Fix:** Map through the piece table instead of casting the index: in RefreshVisibleObjects use `var table = _player.m_buildPieces; if (i >= table.m_categories.Count) { category.SetActive(false); continue; } var buildPieces = table.GetAvailablePiecesInCategory(table.m_categories[i]);`. In Update replace `(int)_player.m_buildPieces.m_selectedCategory` with the list index of the selected category: `var table = _player.m_buildPieces; currentStartIndex = table.m_categories.IndexOf(table.GetSelectedCategory());` (never read m_selectedCategory directly — it is Max until GetSelectedCategory runs). Also guard ToggleBuildMenuTabs against `visibleObject.Value.Value == null` instead of relying on the empty catch.
- **Verifier correction:** Every code fact is confirmed, but the trigger and consequence are not: BuildMenuPaginationController never runs, so nothing is miscomputed at runtime today. The component exists only on HUD.prefab's `hudroot/BuildHud` (MonoBehaviour &1046502534777875257, script fileID 1099087801, guid 045bc81af5d75cb42a79645f3b1c6adb), and Auga instantiates nothing from that branch — every Auga.Assets.Hud use is a Replace/CopyOver of a named hudroot/* path and BuildHud is not one of them. BuildHud.prefab, the asset that does get instantiated (into the inactive dummy at Hud_Setup.cs:147-149), contains no BuildMenuPaginationController at all: grepping it for maximumVisibleTabs/leftPageArrow returns nothing, and HUD.prefab's PrefabInstance overrides for those properties target fileID 7422707394694791295, which does not exist in BuildHud.prefab — stale overrides. So this is a latent defect in dead code, severity 'missing' at most, not 'broken'; the `catch (Exception) {}` in Update never swallows anything because Update never ticks.
- **Verifier's better fix:** The mapping fix is correct if the component is revived. But decide that first: since Auga currently restyles the live vanilla tab strip in place (Hud_Setup.cs:160-190) and that strip already carries all six 1.0 tabs, the cheaper resolution is to delete BuildMenuPaginationController and BuildHudTabFixer along with HUD.prefab's stale overrides rather than port their index arithmetic.

### [inventory-3] Grid replacement drops m_onReleased, m_onEnter, OnSetTouchSelection and the OnMoveTo{Upper,Lower}InventoryGrid callbacks

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 90)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:27-30 (nulls m_onSelected/m_onRightClick on the vanilla grids) and :42-44, :51-53 (re-attaches only those two on the new grids)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.Awake — scratchpad\decomp\InventoryGui.cs:382-403; consumers InventoryGrid.OnDragEnd/OnReleasedOn/OnPointerEnter (decomp\InventoryGrid.cs:497-518), InventoryGrid.UpdateGamepad (decomp\InventoryGrid.cs:136,156), InventoryGrid.SetTouchSelection (decomp\InventoryGrid.cs:612-617)
- **Trigger:** Gamepad: pushing the stick/d-pad past the top row of the container grid or the bottom row of the player grid to jump between the two grids. Touch: dragging an item onto a slot, hovering a slot while dragging, tapping a slot to select it.
- **Consequence:** No exception (all four are invoked with `?.Invoke`), but the features are silently dead: gamepad cannot move the selection between the player grid and an open container (MoveToUpper/MoveToLowerInventoryGrid never fire, so SetActiveGroup is never called and the selection sticks at the grid edge); touch drag-release does nothing (m_onReleased -> InventoryGui.OnReleasedItem); the touch drag effects at InventoryGui.OnEnterElement (decomp\InventoryGui.cs:1011-1021) never spawn; and tapping a slot never switches the active UI group (OnSetTouchSelection -> InventoryGui.SetTouchSelection).
- **Evidence:** Vanilla wires six delegates per grid at InventoryGui.cs:381-407 (`playerGrid2.m_onReleased`, `playerGrid4.m_onEnter`, `playerGrid5.OnMoveToLowerInventoryGrid`, `containerGrid5.OnMoveToUpperInventoryGrid`, `playerGrid6.OnSetTouchSelection`, `playerGrid7.CanDropDragOntoItem`). Auga only writes `__instance.m_playerGrid.m_onSelected += __instance.OnSelectedItem;` and `m_onRightClick += __instance.OnRightClickItem;` (PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:43-44, 52-53). `grep -rn "m_onReleased\|m_onEnter\|OnSetTouchSelection\|OnMoveToUpperInventoryGrid\|OnMoveToLowerInventoryGrid" --include=*.cs` over the whole Auga tree returns no hits. m_onReleased, m_onEnter and OnSetTouchSelection are new in 1.0 (auga_audit\diffs\InventoryGrid.diff `+` lines; auga_field_diff.csv InventoryGrid NewIn10 column); OnMoveToUpper/LowerInventoryGrid already existed in 0.217.30, so that half is a pre-existing Auga gap rather than a 1.0 regression.
- **Fix:** Add the missing bindings in the same postfix block, e.g. `grid.m_onReleased += __instance.OnReleasedItem; grid.m_onEnter += __instance.OnEnterElement; grid.OnSetTouchSelection += __instance.SetTouchSelection;` for both grids, plus `__instance.m_playerGrid.OnMoveToLowerInventoryGrid += __instance.MoveToLowerInventoryGrid;` and `__instance.m_containerGrid.OnMoveToUpperInventoryGrid += __instance.MoveToUpperInventoryGrid;`. Or copy the whole delegate set off the vanilla grid before replacing it.
- **Verifier's better fix:** The listed fix is right but incomplete: also add `grid.CanDropDragOntoItem += __instance.CanDropDragOntoItem;` for both grids. Unlike the other five, 1.0 invokes it WITHOUT a null-conditional — `element.m_canBeDroppedOn = CanDropDragOntoItem(item);` at decomp/InventoryGrid.cs:375 — so a null there is an NRE inside UpdateGui, not a silent loss. That is a candidate for the NullReferenceException still thrown from DMD<InventoryGrid::UpdateGui> in auga_run4.log:337-341. Also note vanilla wires seven delegates per grid, not six.

### [hud-build-2] Auga's category-tab restyle loop is a no-op on 1.0: the tab Buttons moved from Categories/* to Categories/Tabs/*

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 90)
- **Auga:** Auga/Hud_Setup.cs:162-191 (`var categories = __instance.m_pieceSelectionWindow.transform.Find("Categories"); for (i..categories.childCount) { if (!child.TryGetComponent<Button>(out var button)) continue; ... }`)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud build-piece category tabs, hudroot/BuildHud/bar/SelectionWindow/Categories (dumps/Hud_vanilla.txt:111-137); consumed by Hud.UpdateBuild (decomp/Hud.cs:1462-1482)
- **Trigger:** Runs once in Hud_Awake_Postfix at world load.
- **Consequence:** No exception, but the loop body never executes, so the category tabs keep vanilla fonts, colours, font sizes and materials and the 'Selected' highlight image is never made transparent. Even if finding 1 were fixed by re-activating the legacy window, the tabs would still look like stock Valheim.
- **Evidence:** `Categories` has exactly two direct children in 1.0 - `dumps/Hud_vanilla.txt:112` `Categories/TabBorder  [Image]` and `:113` `Categories/Tabs  [HorizontalLayoutGroup]` - and neither carries a `Button`. The six real tabs are one level deeper: `Categories/Tabs/Misc  [Image, UIInputHandler, Button]` (:114), `Tabs/Crafting` (:118), `Tabs/Building` (:122), `Tabs/Building2` (:126), `Tabs/Furniture` (:130), `Tabs/Deep North` (:134), each with `Text` and `Selected/Text` children. Auga's `child.Find("Text")` / `child.Find("Selected")` would only resolve against those deeper objects. Auga's own mirror prefab already uses the nested form (`Hud_Setup.cs:150` looks up `"DividerLarge/TabContainer/Tabs/Misc"` in BuildHud.prefab), which is why the loop was never updated.
- **Fix:** Change the lookup to `m_pieceSelectionWindow.transform.Find("Categories/Tabs")` (and keep the `TryGetComponent<Button>` guard), or better, iterate `Hud.m_pieceCategoryTabs` directly - it is the authoritative array the game itself drives - instead of walking children by transform path.

### [hud-build-5] SetupPieceInfo replacement passes craft:false instead of 1.0's piece.FreeBuildKey() == GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost, so requirement amounts flash red under the no-craft-cost key

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 90)
- **Auga:** Auga/Hud_Setup.cs:382 (`InventoryGui.SetupRequirement(instance.m_requirementItems[index].transform, resource, localPlayer, false, 0);`) inside Hud_SetupPieceInfo_Patch.SetupPieceInfo, which a Transpiler substitutes for the whole vanilla body (Auga/Hud_Setup.cs:418-448)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud.SetupPieceInfo (scratchpad/decomp/Hud.cs:1539) and InventoryGui.SetupRequirement (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1736, 1759)
- **Trigger:** Hovering or selecting any build piece while the `nocraftcost` / NoCraftCost global key is set - in particular the new food/feast/mead build categories, and any piece with an ItemDrop or Feast component.
- **Consequence:** The resource amounts in the piece-info panel keep pulsing red as if unaffordable, even though the piece is free. This panel (hudroot/BuildHud/SelectedInfo) is live in place mode, so it is visible every time the player builds.
- **Evidence:** 1.0 changed the call to `InventoryGui.SetupRequirement(m_requirementItems[j].transform, req, localPlayer, piece.FreeBuildKey() == GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost, 0);` (decomp/Hud.cs:1539); the 0.217.30 line was `... localPlayer, craft: false, 0)` (decomp_old_assembly_valheim/Hud.cs:1320), which is what Auga still copies. The `craft` flag picks which global key suppresses the red flash: `if (num < num2 && ((!craft && !ZoneSystem.instance.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys.NoBuildCost)) || (craft && !ZoneSystem.instance.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost))))` (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1759). `Piece.FreeBuildKey()` returns `GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost` when the piece has an `ItemDrop` or `Feast` component and `GlobalKeys.NoBuildCost` otherwise (decomp/Piece.cs:630-637) - exactly the pieces in the new Feasts/Food/Meads categories (decomp/Piece.cs:61-63).
- **Fix:** In Auga/Hud_Setup.cs:382 pass `piece.FreeBuildKey() == GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost` as the `craft` argument, matching decomp/Hud.cs:1539. (The 1.0 signature gained an optional `int craftMultiplier = 1`, so the existing 5-argument call still binds.)

### [text-chat-2] DamageText_Setup postfix applies the mySelf colour to every TextType, overwriting 1.0's new Bonus/Blocked/TooHard colours - craft and harvest "+N" bonuses turn red

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 90)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\DamageText_Setup.cs:31-40 (`if (type == DamageText.TextType.Heal) ... else if (mySelf) { color = text != "0" ? Auga.Colors.PlayerDamage : Auga.Colors.PlayerNoDamage; } else switch (type) {...}`)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** DamageText.AddInworldText (scratchpad\decomp\DamageText.cs:105-115)
- **Trigger:** Craft or upgrade an item with a crafting bonus, harvest a Pickable with a bonus, or take a cooking-station bonus - all of which call `DamageText.instance.ShowText(DamageText.TextType.Bonus, pos, "+N", player: true)`.
- **Consequence:** The floating "+N" bonus is drawn in Auga.Colors.PlayerDamage (player-damage red) instead of 1.0's orange (1, 0.63, 0.24). Same inversion for TextType.Blocked and TextType.TooHard whenever mySelf is true. No exception.
- **Evidence:** 1.0 restricts the mySelf colours by enum value: `((mySelf && type <= TextType.Immune) ? ((!(text == "0")) ? new Color(1f,0f,0f,1f) : new Color(0.5f,0.5f,0.5f,1f)) : (type switch { ... TextType.Bonus => new Color(1f, 0.63f, 0.24f, 1f), TextType.Heal => ..., _ => Color.white }))`. The enum order is Normal=0, Resistant=1, Weak=2, Immune=3, Heal=4, TooHard=5, Blocked=6, Bonus=7, so 1.0 never reds out 5/6/7. Auga's postfix only carves out Heal before the `else if (mySelf)` branch. Callers passing player:true with Bonus: InventoryGui.cs:1921 `DamageText.instance.ShowText(DamageText.TextType.Bonus, vector, $"+{num4}", player: true)`, Pickable.cs:219, CookingStation.cs:665.
- **Fix:** Mirror the 1.0 guard: change `else if (mySelf)` to `else if (mySelf && type <= DamageText.TextType.Immune)` and add explicit `case DamageText.TextType.Bonus:` / `case DamageText.TextType.Blocked:` arms to the switch (Bonus should stay orange).
- **Verifier's better fix:** The fix is correct. One scope note for the write-up: in the shipped 1.0 code no caller passes player:true for TextType.Blocked (Humanoid.cs:1801) or TextType.TooHard (Destructible.cs:119, MineRock.cs:143, MineRock5.cs:379, TreeBase.cs:119, TreeLog.cs:171, WearNTear.cs:1217), so the observable regression is confined to the Bonus "+N" text; the Blocked/TooHard half is defensive only.

### [auga-lib-2] AugaCraftingPanel drives the craft progress bar with m_craftTimer / m_craftDuration; 1.0 computes the duration from three other fields plus the crafting skill

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 90)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/AugaCraftingPanel.cs:177-188 (OnUpdateRecipe), called from Auga/PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:250
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.UpdateRecipe (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1625-1637)
- **Trigger:** Craft anything at a station with a crafting skill, multi-craft a stack, or use an upgrader station — watch the progress bar.
- **Consequence:** The bar's fill is wrong for the whole craft. With the crafting skill raised the real duration shrinks by up to 60%, so the bar sits far behind and the item pops at ~40% fill; multi-crafting (m_multiCraftDuration = 6s) and upgrading (m_upgraderDuration = 8s + 1s/level) run 3-4x longer than m_craftDuration = 2f, so the bar hits 100% and then clamps for the rest of the craft. Vanilla's own bar cannot compensate because Auga redirects it to a throwaway (`__instance.m_craftProgressBar = CraftingPanel.DummyCraftProgressBar;`, Auga/PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:115).
- **Evidence:** Auga: `var percent = instance.m_craftTimer / instance.m_craftDuration; CraftProgressBar.fillAmount = percent;` (AugaCraftingPanel.cs:182-183). 0.217 matched this exactly: `m_craftProgressBar.SetMaxValue(m_craftDuration); ... if (m_craftTimer >= m_craftDuration)` (decomp_old_assembly_valheim/InventoryGui.cs:1276,1279). 1.0: `float num6 = (flag ? (m_upgraderDuration + (float)num * m_upgraderDurationPerLevel) : (m_multiCrafting ? m_multiCraftDuration : m_craftDuration)); if (currentCraftingStation != null && currentCraftingStation.m_craftingSkill != Skills.SkillType.None) { num6 *= 1f - Player.m_localPlayer.GetSkillFactor(currentCraftingStation.m_craftingSkill) * m_craftDurationSkillMaxDecrease; } ... m_craftProgressBar.SetMaxValue(num6);` (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1625-1632). `m_multiCraftDuration`, `m_upgraderDuration`, `m_upgraderDurationPerLevel`, `m_craftDurationSkillMaxDecrease`, `m_multiCrafting` and `CraftingStation.m_craftingSkill` all return 0 hits in the 0.217 decompile.
- **Fix:** Replicate 1.0's duration in OnUpdateRecipe, or better: stop redirecting m_craftProgressBar to a dummy and let vanilla drive Auga's bar. Minimal fix — compute `float dur = instance.m_upgraderActive ? (instance.m_upgraderDuration + level * instance.m_upgraderDurationPerLevel) : (instance.m_multiCrafting ? instance.m_multiCraftDuration : instance.m_craftDuration); var st = Player.m_localPlayer.GetCurrentCraftingStation(); if (st != null && st.m_craftingSkill != Skills.SkillType.None) dur *= 1f - Player.m_localPlayer.GetSkillFactor(st.m_craftingSkill) * instance.m_craftDurationSkillMaxDecrease;` and divide by that.
- **Verifier's better fix:** The approach is right but the code sketch will not compile: there is no `InventoryGui.m_upgraderActive`. 1.0 derives it per call as `bool flag = (bool)currentCraftingStation && currentCraftingStation.m_upgrader;` (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1481), and the per-level term multiplies the *quality* level `num` from UpdateRecipe, not an item level. So: `var st = Player.m_localPlayer.GetCurrentCraftingStation(); bool upg = st != null && st.m_upgrader; float dur = upg ? (instance.m_upgraderDuration + quality * instance.m_upgraderDurationPerLevel) : (instance.m_multiCrafting ? instance.m_multiCraftDuration : instance.m_craftDuration);` then the skill factor. The preferred option (stop redirecting m_craftProgressBar to the dummy) is the better one and avoids re-deriving anything.

### [pause-texts-2] Auga's SkipIntro menu entry is permanently visible and its onClick calls Menu.OnManualSave instead of Menu.OnSkip

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 88)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnity\Assets\Prefabs\AugaMenu.prefab, PrefabInstance &7881465935064588165 (m_Name SkipIntro, ~lines 2955-3216)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Menu.SetButtonsEnabled() (decomp\Menu.cs:250) and Menu.OnSkip() (decomp\Menu.cs:405)
- **Trigger:** Open the pause menu at any time; the 'Skip intro' entry is listed. Click it.
- **Consequence:** A 'Skip intro' entry is shown permanently (outside the intro) and clicking it performs a manual world save instead of skipping the intro. Conversely, during the actual intro there is no working way to skip it from the Auga menu. Game.instance.SkipIntro() (new in 1.0, replacing RequestRespawn+Valkyrie.DropPlayer) is never reachable.
- **Evidence:** AugaMenu.prefab PrefabInstance &7881465935064588165 carries `propertyPath: m_Name / value: SkipIntro` (line 2968), `propertyPath: m_IsActive / value: 1` (line 2974) and `...m_OnClick.m_PersistentCalls.m_Calls.Array.data[0].m_MethodName / value: OnManualSave` (line 3033) - the MenuButton was duplicated from the Save entry. 0.217 hid it via `m_skipButton.gameObject.SetActive(Player.m_localPlayer && Player.m_localPlayer.InIntro())` (decomp_old_assembly_valheim\Menu.cs:201); 1.0 does it in SetButtonsEnabled, which never completes (see the m_skipButton finding).
- **Fix:** Change that PrefabInstance's persistent call to `OnSkip` and set its `m_IsActive` override to 0, so 1.0's `SetButtonsEnabled` (once it stops throwing) owns its visibility via `Game.instance.InIntro(includeQueued: true) || Player.m_localPlayer.InIntro()`.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Also repair the reference type: change `m_skipButton` on AugaMenu.prefab (line 450) from the stripped GameObject 4824936238869214461 to the Button component on that SkipIntro instance (the way saveButton at :437 and menuCurrentPlayersListButton at :436 point at !u!114 stripped MonoBehaviours). Without that, SetButtonsEnabled keeps throwing on its first line and nothing else in the menu gets its visibility set either.

### [text-chat-1] AugaTextInput never wires GuiInputField.OnInputSubmit -> TextInput.OnInput, and 1.0 deleted the Enter handling from TextInput.Update, so Enter no longer confirms sign/pet/portal text

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 88)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnity\Assets\Prefabs\AugaTextInput.prefab (GuiInputField &7712704899483783889 on panel/TextField, a nested instance of TextField.prefab guid ae0d5c8245b17784e9c15381084fd582); the only two UnityEvent wirings in the whole prefab are at lines 1139-1140 (m_MethodName: OnCancel) and 1324-1325 (m_MethodName: OnEnter), both m_OnClick on the Cancel/OK buttons
- **Vanilla 1.0:** TextInput.Update and TextInput.OnInput (scratchpad\decomp\TextInput.cs:41-61); old body at scratchpad\decomp_old_assembly_valheim\TextInput.cs:41-55
- **Trigger:** Interact with a sign, a tamed animal or a portal, type a name, press Enter (or Numpad Enter).
- **Consequence:** Nothing happens - the text is never committed and the panel stays open. The only way to confirm is to click the OK button. No exception, so nothing shows in the log.
- **Evidence:** 0.217.30 TextInput.Update: `else if (ZInput.GetKeyDown((KeyCode)13) || ZInput.GetKeyDown((KeyCode)271)) { OnEnter(); }`. 1.0 TextInput.Update: `if (m_visibleFrame && !Console.IsVisible() && !Chat.instance.HasFocus() && ZInput.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Escape)) { Hide(); }` - Return/KeypadEnter gone, replaced by the new `public void OnInput() { setText(m_inputField.text...); m_bShouldHideNextFrame = true; }`, which no C# code calls (grep over decomp\*.cs: the only hit is its own declaration at TextInput.cs:58), so it can only be driven from the prefab's GuiInputField event. TextField.prefab lines 359-361: `OnInputSubmit:\n    m_PersistentCalls:\n      m_Calls: []`, and AugaTextInput.prefab adds no `propertyPath: OnInputSubmit...` override (grep: 0 hits for OnInputSubmit / m_OnSubmit / m_OnEndEdit). Contrast AugaChat, where the same empty OnInputSubmit is compensated in code by AugaUnityLib\ChatWindowController.cs:18 `_chatHandler.m_input.OnInputSubmit.AddListener(AddToChatOutput);` - TextInput has no such helper.
- **Fix:** Either add a persistent call OnInputSubmit -> TextInput.OnInput (m_TargetAssemblyTypeName "TextInput, assembly_valheim", target = the TextInput component &4343464309352343074) on the AugaTextInput GuiInputField, or add it at runtime in a TextInput_Setup postfix: `__instance.m_inputField.OnInputSubmit.AddListener(_ => __instance.OnInput());` (mirroring ChatWindowController.Awake).
- **Verifier's better fix:** The runtime variant is the safer of the two, since it survives a prefab rebuild. Note AugaTextInput's TextField also carries a GuiInputFieldSubmit (from TextField.prefab), whose m_onSubmit is null here — if you instead revive that component you get a null-delegate NRE, so wire OnInputSubmit and leave GuiInputFieldSubmit alone.

### [world-ui-4] EnemyHud_ShowHud_Patch's guard is inverted, so Auga's level_4/5/6 and level_X star displays are unreachable dead code

- **Verdict:** partly (reviewer confidence 88)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\EnemeyHud_Setup.cs:25-28 (`if (c == null || __instance.m_huds.TryGetValue(c, out EnemyHud.HudData _)) { return; }`)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** EnemyHud.ShowHud (decomp\EnemyHud.cs:126-158), whose last statement is `m_huds.Add(c, value);`
- **Trigger:** Any creature with level > 3 (3+ stars) coming into hud range - e.g. with CreatureLevelControl, or any world modifier/mod that raises star counts.
- **Consequence:** The postfix returns immediately on every invocation, because ShowHud has just added `c` to m_huds so TryGetValue always succeeds. The extended star artwork Auga ships (HudBase/level_4, level_5, level_6, level_X with its "x N" Level text, and the same sets on HudBaseBoss and HudMount) is never activated and the level_X clone / `text.text = $"x {level - 1}"` code never runs, so 3+ star creatures show no stars at all.
- **Evidence:** AugaEnemyHud.prefab contains HudRoot/HudBase/level_4, level_5, level_6 and level_X (with a child "Level" TextMeshProUGUI), plus the same sets under HudBaseBoss and HudMount, all `active=0` in the prefab and only ever enabled by this postfix. 1.0's ShowHud is unchanged in this respect (diff hunk @@ -131,128 +126,97 @@ only rewrites ILSpy casting noise) and still ends with `m_huds.Add(c, value);`, so the TryGetValue can never fail. The loop body is independently wrong too: `var levelDisplay = hudGui.transform.Find($"level_{level}");` uses `level` instead of the loop variable `i`, and `levelDisplayX.GetComponentInChildren<Text>()` looks for a legacy UnityEngine.UI.Text while level_X/Level carries a TextMeshProUGUI (script guid 5f7201a1...), so it would NRE if it ever ran. This predates the 1.0 port but it is a dead feature in the current build.
- **Fix:** Negate the guard (`if (c == null || !__instance.m_huds.TryGetValue(c, out var data)) return;`), use `data` instead of `m_huds.LastOrDefault()` - dictionary enumeration order is not insertion order once 1.0's new EnemyHud.RemoveCharacterHud, called from Character.OnDestroy at decomp\Character.cs:763, has removed entries - change `Find($"level_{level}")` to `Find($"level_{i}")`, and change `GetComponentInChildren<Text>()` to `GetComponentInChildren<TMPro.TMP_Text>()`.
- **Verifier correction:** The dead-code core is right, but two supporting details are wrong. (1) level_X/Level does NOT carry a TextMeshProUGUI: in AugaEnemyHud.prefab it carries guid 5f7201a12d95ffc409449d95f23cf332, whose component block serializes m_FontData/m_Font — that is UnityEngine.UI.Text, so `GetComponentInChildren<Text>()` is the correct type. It would still NRE, but for a different reason: level_X is inactive in the prefab and nothing in this code path activates it, and GetComponentInChildren<T>() without includeInactive:true returns null on an inactive branch. (2) The `Find($"level_{level}")` bug is real but its effect is not "never activated": the same object is fetched every iteration and SetActive(i == level) is called five times, so the last iteration (i = 6) leaves level_4 and level_5 switched OFF and only level_6 on.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Negate the guard and use the `data` out-value as proposed, and change `Find($"level_{level}")` to `Find($"level_{i}")`. But keep `GetComponentInChildren<Text>()` (the component is UnityEngine.UI.Text) and pass includeInactive: true — `levelDisplayX.GetComponentInChildren<Text>(true)` — or activate level_X before reading it; switching to TMP_Text as the finding suggests would break it.

### [harmony-8] Hud.SetupPieceInfo transpiler re-implementation drops 1.0's free-build-key handling, the $menu_none localization, and leaves a stale snapping icon

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 88)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\Hud_Setup.cs:349-448 (Hud_SetupPieceInfo_Patch.SetupPieceInfo + Transpiler that replaces the whole body with a call to it)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud.SetupPieceInfo (decomp\Hud.cs:1511-1572) and InventoryGui.SetupRequirement (decomp\InventoryGui.cs:1736-1768)
- **Trigger:** Enter build mode and hover/select any piece; most visible on a world with the `nocraftcost` global key set, or with any station-requiring piece.
- **Consequence:** (a) requirement amounts flash red even when the world's NoCraftCost key is set, because Auga hardcodes `craft: false` so SetupRequirement checks GlobalKeys.NoBuildCost instead; (b) the missing-station label reads the literal English 'None' instead of the localized '$menu_none'; (c) the station-missing text flashes red even with NoCraftCost set; (d) the snapping icon is never turned off - once shown it stays on the last piece's sprite.
- **Evidence:** 1.0: `InventoryGui.SetupRequirement(m_requirementItems[j].transform, req, localPlayer, piece.FreeBuildKey() == GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost, 0);` vs Auga `InventoryGui.SetupRequirement(instance.m_requirementItems[index].transform, resource, localPlayer, false, 0);`. SetupRequirement branches on that flag: `if (num < num2 && ((!craft && !ZoneSystem.instance.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys.NoBuildCost)) || (craft && !ZoneSystem.instance.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost))))`. 1.0: `component3.text = Localization.instance.Localize("$menu_none"); component3.color = ((Mathf.Sin(Time.time * 10f) > 0f && !ZoneSystem.instance.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost)) ? Color.red : Color.white);` vs Auga `component3.text = "None"; component3.color = Mathf.Sin(Time.time * 10f) > 0.0 ? Color.red : Color.white;`. 1.0 always writes `m_snappingIcon.sprite = snappingIconForPiece; m_snappingIcon.enabled = snappingIconForPiece != null && (...)` whereas Auga wraps both in `if (snappingIconForPiece != null)`, and GetSnappingIconForPiece returns null whenever `!Player.m_localPlayer.AlternativePlacementActive`. (Auga did correctly pick up 1.0's category rename Building -> BuildingWorkbench.)
- **Fix:** Pass `piece.FreeBuildKey() == GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost` as the craft flag, use `Localization.instance.Localize("$menu_none")`, add the `&& !ZoneSystem.instance.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost)` term, and move the snapping-icon sprite/enabled assignment out of the null check (keep only the `instance.m_snappingIcon != null` guard).
- **Verifier's better fix:** Also add a null guard on `instance.m_snappingIcon` when moving the assignment out of the `snappingIconForPiece != null` check — the existing null-piece branch at Hud_Setup.cs:357 already guards with `if (instance.m_snappingIcon != null)`, but line 373 dereferences it unguarded today, so the two paths should be made consistent.

### [harmony-4] No prefix for the new Hud.UpdateAdrenaline, so the untouched vanilla adrenaline bar keeps drawing over Auga's HUD

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 87)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\Hud_Setup.cs:90-131 (Awake postfix destroys/nulls the health, stamina, food and eitr bars) and Hud_Setup.cs:230-277 (prefixes for UpdateStatusEffects, UpdateFood, SetHealthBarSize, SetStaminaBarSize, UpdateHealth, UpdateStamina, UpdateEitr - there is no UpdateAdrenaline prefix)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud.UpdateAdrenaline (decomp\Hud.cs:1122-1161), called every frame from Hud.Update; Hud.SetAdrenalineBarSize; new fields m_adrenalineBarRoot/m_adrenalineAnimator/m_adrenalineBarFast/m_adrenalineBarSlow/m_adrenalineText
- **Trigger:** Gain any adrenaline (1.0 combat mechanic) while playing with Auga.
- **Consequence:** Vanilla's adrenaline panel is animated and positioned by vanilla code (anchoredPosition (0,130) or (0,320)) on top of Auga's re-laid-out HUD, in vanilla art, un-movable by MovableHudElement; Auga has no adrenaline bar of its own, so the value is shown twice-styled/out of place. No exception (the panel is a sibling, not a child of m_healthPanel, so Auga's destroy loop misses it).
- **Evidence:** Auga's Awake postfix does `foreach (Transform child in __instance.m_healthPanel) Object.Destroy(child.gameObject);` plus `Object.Destroy(__instance.m_staminaBar2Root.gameObject); Object.Destroy(__instance.m_eitrBarRoot.gameObject);` and then nulls m_healthBarRoot/m_foodBars/m_staminaBar2Root/m_eitrBarRoot etc., but never touches m_adrenaline*. dumps\Hud_vanilla.txt lines 15-24 show `hudroot/adrenalinepanel [Animator]`, `hudroot/adrenalinepanel/Stamina/adrenaline_slow [GuiBar]`, `.../adrenaline_fast [GuiBar]`, `.../adrenalineText [TextMeshProUGUI]` - a sibling of `hudroot/healthpanel` (line 35), so it survives. auga_field_diff.csv Hud row lists m_adrenalineBarRoot, m_adrenalineAnimator, m_adrenalineBarFast, m_adrenalineBarSlow, m_adrenalineText as New in 1.0.
- **Fix:** Add `[HarmonyPatch(nameof(Hud.UpdateAdrenaline))] [HarmonyPrefix] static bool Prefix() => false;` alongside the other bar prefixes and destroy/hide `hudroot/adrenalinepanel` in the Awake postfix, or build an Auga adrenaline bar (CopyOver an AugaHealthBar like the eitr bar) and re-point m_adrenalineAnimator/m_adrenalineBar*/m_adrenalineText at it.

### [crafting-1] m_uiGroups[2] and [3] are the same UIGroupHandler, so 1.0's SetActiveGroup(m_uiGroups[3]) deactivates the crafting panel's UI group on every recipe/craft/repair/tab click

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 85)
- **Auga:** Auga/PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:132-139 (m_uiGroups = { container, player, rightPanel.GetComponent<UIGroupHandler>(), rightPanel.GetComponent<UIGroupHandler>() })
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.SetActiveGroup(UIGroupHandler) (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:622-625), SetActiveGroup(int) loop (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:641-645), callers at :1390 OnSelectedRecipe, :2061 OnCraftPressed, :2111 OnRepairPressed, :2563 OnTabCraftPressed, :2571 OnTabUpgradePressed; UpdateGamepad's `if (m_activeGroup == 3) UpdateRecipeGamepadInput();` (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:615-618)
- **Trigger:** Click any recipe in the list, the Craft button, the Repair button, or the Craft/Upgrade tab while the inventory is open.
- **Consequence:** `SetActiveGroup(UIGroupHandler)` is new in 1.0 and resolves the index with `Array.IndexOf(m_uiGroups, group)`, which returns 2 for the duplicated rightPanel handler, never 3. m_activeGroup becomes 2, and because slots 2 and 3 hold the SAME component the loop's last write wins: `m_uiGroups[3].SetActive(false)` turns the right panel's UIGroupHandler off immediately after `m_uiGroups[2].SetActive(true)` turned it on. The crafting panel's group then reports IsActive==false (gamepad hint object `m_enableWhenActiveAndGamepad` hidden, no default-element focus, and CanvasGroup.interactable forced false if one is present), and m_activeGroup can never reach 3 through any click, so `UpdateRecipeGamepadInput()` (stick / D-pad scrolling of the recipe list) is unreachable except by tabbing right from group 2.
- **Evidence:** Auga: `rightPanel.GetComponent<UIGroupHandler>(), rightPanel.GetComponent<UIGroupHandler>()` - the same component object in both slots (RightPanel.prefab's root carries exactly one UIGroupHandler, script -626263950 guid d920e4723ba2566674efb2f0c3eb6edb). 1.0: `private void SetActiveGroup(UIGroupHandler group, bool playSound = true) { SetActiveGroup(Array.IndexOf(m_uiGroups, group), playSound); }` and `for (int i = 0; i < m_uiGroups.Length; i++) { m_uiGroups[i].SetActive(i == m_activeGroup); }`. 0.217 had no UIGroupHandler overload and no `m_uiGroups[3]` call site at all (decomp_old_assembly_valheim/InventoryGui.cs mentions m_uiGroups only at 469-482, inside SetActiveGroup(int)).
- **Fix:** Give the right panel two distinct UIGroupHandler components (one on the RightPanel root for the info/skills group, one on the crafting sub-branch used as slot 3), or add a Harmony prefix on `InventoryGui.SetActiveGroup(UIGroupHandler)` that maps Auga's shared handler to index 3 for the crafting entry points. Two slots pointing at one component make the SetActive loop cancel itself out.

### [hud-bars-4] Auga's food timers show raw Player.Food.m_time; 1.0 converts to real seconds with food.m_time / Game.m_foodRate

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 85)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnityLib\PlayerPanelFoodController.cs:111 `var secondsRemaining = Mathf.CeilToInt(food.m_time);` (rendered at line 118-122 as `TimeSpan.FromSeconds(secondsRemaining).ToString("m\\:ss")`) and C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnityLib\ComplexTooltip.cs:719 `var currentTime = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(Mathf.CeilToInt(food.m_time)).ToString(...)` / :715 `durationText` from raw m_foodBurnTime
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud.UpdateFood (decomp/Hud.cs:1037) `float num = food.m_time / Game.m_foodRate;` then `tMP_Text.text = Mathf.CeilToInt(num / 60f) + "m"` / `Mathf.FloorToInt(num) + "s"`; the rate comes from Game.m_foodRate (decomp/Game.cs:204) set by `trySetScalarKey(GlobalKeys.FoodRate, out m_foodRate)` in Game.UpdateWorldRates (decomp/Game.cs:1372); Player.UpdateFood (decomp/Player.cs:2417) `m_foodUpdateTimer += dt * Game.m_foodRate`
- **Trigger:** Playing any world created with the 1.0 "Food duration" world modifier set to anything other than 1x, then opening the player/inventory panel food list or hovering a food item's tooltip.
- **Consequence:** No exception. The remaining-time text is wrong by exactly the FoodRate factor — e.g. at 2x food drain m_time falls 2 units per real second, so Auga prints twice the real time left ("20:00" when 10 minutes remain). Auga's own Hud.UpdateFood prefix (Hud_Setup.cs:237-242) returns false, so vanilla's corrected text never renders anywhere as a fallback. The radial CountdownImage fill (PlayerPanelFoodController.cs:110, m_time / m_foodBurnTime) is rate-independent and stays correct, which is why the HUD food icons look fine while the panel text does not.
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn m_foodRate` finds the symbol only in the 1.0 tree (decomp/Game.cs:204,1372; decomp/Hud.cs:1037; decomp/Player.cs:2417) and nowhere in decomp_old_assembly_valheim — it is new in 1.0. Player.Food is byte-identical old-vs-new (m_name, m_item, m_time, m_health, m_stamina, m_eitr, CanEatAgain), so m_time itself did not change meaning; only the conversion to display seconds is new. FoodPanel.prefab binds `TimeRemainingText: {fileID: 261920983954304822}` while FoodPanel_HUD.prefab binds `TimeRemainingText: {fileID: 0}`, so the defect is confined to the player-panel list and the item tooltip.
- **Fix:** Divide by the rate in both places: `var secondsRemaining = Mathf.CeilToInt(food.m_time / Game.m_foodRate);` in PlayerPanelFoodController.UpdateFood, the same in ComplexTooltip.AddFoodTextBox line 719, and `Mathf.CeilToInt(food.m_item.m_shared.m_foodBurnTime / Game.m_foodRate)` for the two total-duration strings (PlayerPanelFoodController.cs:119, ComplexTooltip.cs:715). Guard against m_foodRate == 0.

### [hud-build-7] SetupPieceInfo replacement only writes m_snappingIcon.enabled when a snapping sprite exists, leaving a stale snapping icon on screen

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 85)
- **Auga:** Auga/Hud_Setup.cs:370-375 (`Sprite snappingIconForPiece = instance.GetSnappingIconForPiece(piece); if (snappingIconForPiece != null) { instance.m_snappingIcon.sprite = ...; instance.m_snappingIcon.enabled = ...; }`)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud.SetupPieceInfo (scratchpad/decomp/Hud.cs:1530-1532) and Hud.GetSnappingIconForPiece (decomp/Hud.cs:1574-1597)
- **Trigger:** Hover a build piece with alternative placement active (so a snapping icon shows), then toggle alternative placement off while staying in place mode.
- **Consequence:** `hudroot/BuildHud/SelectedInfo/selected_piece/snappingHint` stays enabled with the previous sprite indefinitely, telling the player snapping is active when it is not. Vanilla clears it on the very next frame.
- **Evidence:** Vanilla assigns unconditionally: `m_snappingIcon.sprite = snappingIconForPiece; m_snappingIcon.enabled = snappingIconForPiece != null && (piece.m_category == Piece.PieceCategory.BuildingWorkbench || piece.m_groundPiece || piece.m_waterPiece);` (decomp/Hud.cs:1531-1532). Auga wraps both lines in `if (snappingIconForPiece != null)`, so the false case never runs. `GetSnappingIconForPiece` returns `null` on every branch when `!Player.m_localPlayer.AlternativePlacementActive` (decomp/Hud.cs:1576-1596). The icon object exists and is live in place mode: dumps/Hud_vanilla.txt:166 `hudroot/BuildHud/SelectedInfo/selected_piece/snappingHint  [Image]`, and PortDiagnostics reports `m_snappingIcon` as assigned (it is not in the 15 unassigned Hud fields in run4 line 256). Note this defect predates 1.0, but 1.0's new `SelectedInfo` panel is the one now on screen and Auga already had to touch this line for the `Building` -> `BuildingWorkbench` rename.
- **Fix:** Hoist the two assignments out of the null check in Auga/Hud_Setup.cs:371-375 so they match decomp/Hud.cs:1531-1532 exactly, keeping only the existing `instance.m_snappingIcon != null` component guard.

### [minimap-1] Map pins can no longer be named: the only path to Minimap.OnPinTextEntered is GuiInputFieldSubmit's legacy UnityEngine.Input.GetKeyDown, which is dead in 1.0

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 85)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnityLib\GuiInputFieldSubmit.cs:17 (wired by Auga\Minimap_Setup.cs:20 via the ShowPinNameInput transpiler)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Minimap.OnPinTextEntered / Minimap.ShowPinNameInput / Minimap.Update (scratchpad\decomp\Minimap.cs:893, :809, :698-705)
- **Trigger:** Open the large map, double-click to drop a pin, type a name, press Enter (or KeypadEnter).
- **Consequence:** OnPinTextEntered is never called, so the pin keeps the empty name AddPin gave it and no PinNameData is created; the field stays open (GuiInputFieldSubmit.Update re-calls ActivateInputField every frame) until Escape, whose handler in Minimap.Update deliberately does m_nameInput.text = ""; OnPinTextEntered(""). Net effect: pins can be placed but never named. If Unity's active input handling is "Input System Package (New)" the call additionally throws InvalidOperationException out of Update once per frame while the box is open.
- **Evidence:** GuiInputFieldSubmit.Update: `if (!(m_field.text != "") || !Input.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Return) && !Input.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.KeypadEnter) && !ZInput.GetButtonDown("JoyButtonA")) return; m_onSubmit(m_field.text);` — `Input` here is UnityEngine.Input (legacy). Valheim 1.0 is new-Input-System only: a grep of scratchpad\decomp\*.cs finds zero legacy `Input.Get*` calls, ZInput (decomp_assembly_utils\ZInput.cs:8-15) is built entirely on UnityEngine.InputSystem, and the user's own sibling mod states "the game no longer uses legacy input, so BepInEx KeyboardShortcut does not work" (Valheim creative\README.md:32). There is no fallback: Auga's TextField.prefab GuiInputField has `m_OnEndEdit: m_PersistentCalls: m_Calls: []` and `m_OnSubmit: m_PersistentCalls: m_Calls: []` (TextField.prefab lines 318-325), and the transpiler only assigns `GuiInputFieldSubmit.m_onSubmit`, never the TMP UnityEvents.
- **Fix:** In GuiInputFieldSubmit.Update replace the legacy checks with the new Input System, e.g. `var kb = UnityEngine.InputSystem.Keyboard.current; if (kb != null && (kb.enterKey.wasPressedThisFrame || kb.numpadEnterKey.wasPressedThisFrame)) ...`, keeping the ZInput.GetButtonDown("JoyButtonA") branch. Safer belt-and-braces: also have MinimapShowPinNameInputTranspiler.AddSubmitAction do `instance.m_nameInput.onSubmit.RemoveAllListeners(); instance.m_nameInput.onSubmit.AddListener(instance.OnPinTextEntered);` so TMP_InputField's own Return handling reaches OnPinTextEntered.

### [pause-texts-9] AugaCompendiumController.Update uses legacy UnityEngine.Input, which is dead in Valheim 1.0 - Escape can no longer close the compendium

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 85)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnityLib\AugaCompendiumController.cs:153 `if (Input.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Escape) || ZInput.GetButtonDown("JoyMenu"))`
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Menu.Update() escape gate (decomp\Menu.cs:328, `... && !m_settingsInstance && !m_currentPlayersInstance && ...`) combined with 1.0's switch to the Input System package
- **Trigger:** Open the pause menu -> Compendium, then press Escape on a keyboard.
- **Consequence:** Nothing happens. `UnityEngine.Input.GetKeyDown` never fires under 1.0's Input-System-only setup, and because ShowCompendium sets `Menu.instance.m_settingsInstance = gameObject` (AugaCompendiumController.cs:142), Menu.Update's own Escape handler is also gated off. The player must find and click the compendium's HideCompendium button (Compendium.prefab:5118); gamepad users still escape via 'JoyMenu'.
- **Evidence:** A grep for `Input.(GetKey|GetButton|GetAxis|GetMouse|mousePosition)` over scratchpad\decomp returns zero hits, while decomp_old_assembly_valheim has many (Chat.cs:209, GameCamera.cs:278, KeyHints.cs:148, ManageSavesMenu.cs:90...) - 1.0 removed every legacy Input call. The project memory note valheim-1-0-modding-setup.md states "Valheim 1.0 uses Unity's new Input System only (Unity.InputSystem.dll, no legacy Input.GetKey calls anywhere in game code). BepInEx KeyboardShortcut/UnityInput hotkeys silently never fire." No exception appears in any run log for the identical calls in ChatWindowController.cs:23-24, confirming silent-false rather than throwing.
- **Fix:** Replace with `ZInput.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Escape) || ZInput.GetButtonDown("JoyMenu")` (ZInput routes through the new Input System). The same swap is needed in ChatWindowController.cs:23-24, GuiInputFieldSubmit.cs:17 and InputFieldSubmitEvents.cs:23 (other areas).

### [main-menu-1] Connection_Setup is the only replacement left in the port that hard-destroys the vanilla object instead of keeping a donor

- **Verdict:** partly (reviewer confidence 85)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\Connection_Setup.cs:13-22
- **Vanilla 1.0:** ZNet.Awake / ZNet.RPC_ClientHandshake (decomp/ZNet.cs:333, 908-930)
- **Trigger:** Every game start (ZNet.Awake). Visible when joining any password-protected or slow-to-connect server.
- **Consequence:** The vanilla PasswordDialog and ConnectingDialog GameObjects are permanently destroyed with no fallback: anything wired inside them in the scene (buttons and their UnityEvents, UIGroupHandler registration, any serialized reference another 1.0 component holds into that branch) is gone, and no null/empty field on Auga's copies is filled from a donor. Auga's two prefabs contain no Button at all, so whatever OK/Cancel affordance vanilla's dialogs had is lost - the only way out of the password prompt is Enter via GuiInputField.OnInputSubmit.
- **Evidence:** Connection_Setup.cs uses raw `Object.Destroy(__instance.m_passwordDialog.gameObject)` and `Object.Destroy(__instance.m_connectingDialog.gameObject)` followed by `Object.Instantiate(Auga.Assets.PasswordDialog, parent, false)`. Every other swap in the port goes through `Extensions.Replace` (Extensions.cs:62-110), which since the port calls `PortCarryOver.MakeDonor(foundOriginal.gameObject)` and `PortCarryOver.InstantiateFilled(..., donor)`. Full child list of both Auga prefabs (rendered from the YAML): AugaPassword = Topic, Text, gamepad_hint, Darken, panel, Background, DividerMedium, TextFieldLegacy; AugaConnecting = Topic, Darken, panel, Background, DividerMedium, LoadingEye. No `UnityEngine.UI.Button` (guid 4e29b1a8efbd4b44bb3f3716e73f07ff) appears in either file. Note also that `parent` is taken only from `m_passwordDialog.parent` and reused for the connecting dialog, so if 1.0 parents them differently the connecting screen is re-homed.
- **Fix:** Replace the two Destroy/Instantiate pairs with `PortCarryOver.MakeDonor(...)` + `PortCarryOver.InstantiateFilled(Auga.Assets.PasswordDialog, parent, donor)` (or just call `__instance.Replace(...)` if the dialogs can be found by path), and take each dialog's own `.parent` rather than reusing the password dialog's.
- **Verifier correction:** The mechanism is real but "the only replacement left in the port that hard-destroys the vanilla object" is false: Hud_Setup.cs:92-95 also raw-destroys (`foreach (Transform child in __instance.m_healthPanel) Object.Destroy(child.gameObject); Object.Destroy(__instance.m_staminaBar2Root.gameObject); Object.Destroy(__instance.m_eitrBarRoot.gameObject);`) with no donor. (MainMenu_Setup.cs:118's `Object.Destroy(__instance.m_joinIPPanel)` does not count — it sits inside the block comment that runs from MainMenu_Setup.cs:37 to :195.) The consequence is also over-asserted: the materials contain no hierarchy dump for ZNet's PasswordDialog/ConnectingDialog, so "buttons and their UnityEvents, UIGroupHandler registration" being lost is unverified — and 1.0's only code path through the password dialog is `m_passwordDialog.GetComponentInChildren<GuiInputField>()` + OnInputSubmit (decomp/ZNet.cs:914-936), which needs no button.
- **Verifier's better fix:** The fix (MakeDonor + InstantiateFilled, and take each dialog's own `.parent`) is correct. Drop the "only one" framing and apply the same treatment to Hud_Setup.cs:92-95 if anything in 1.0 still points into the health/stamina/eitr bar branches.

### [auga-lib-3] AugaCraftingPanel's requirement wireframe ignores 1.0's m_upgraderResource and m_requireOnlyOneIngredient filtering and the multi-craft multiplier

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 85)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/AugaCraftingPanel.cs:205-273 (HaveRequirementsHelper and PostSetupRequirementList, esp. the `foreach (var resource in recipe.m_resources)` at :247), called from Auga/PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:274
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.SetupRequirementList (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1642-1696), Piece.Requirement.m_upgraderResource / GetAmount (decomp/Piece.cs)
- **Trigger:** Open the crafting panel at an upgrader station, or select any recipe with m_requireOnlyOneIngredient, or multi-craft.
- **Consequence:** The wireframe pips disagree with the requirement rows vanilla actually filled in: pips are drawn for resources 1.0 filtered out (non-upgrader resources at an upgrader station, or all of the alternative ingredients of a require-only-one recipe instead of the single one that matters), and the 'can craft' colour on FinalWire is computed from that wrong set — it can show red while the craft button is enabled, or green while it is not. With multi-craft the required amounts are not multiplied, so requirements that are short show as satisfied.
- **Evidence:** Auga iterates the raw array: `foreach (var resource in recipe.m_resources) { var amountRequired = resource.GetAmount(quality); if (resource.m_resItem != null && amountRequired > 0) { var have = HaveRequirementsHelper(player, new[] { resource }, quality); ... } }` (AugaCraftingPanel.cs:247-257) and HaveRequirementsHelper compares `player.m_inventory.CountItems(...)` to `resource.GetAmount(qualityLevel)` with no multiplier (:205-218). 1.0 builds m_reqList with two extra gates: `if ((!(currentCraftingStation != null) || currentCraftingStation.m_upgrader == requirement.m_upgraderResource) && (!(currentCraftingStation == null) || !requirement.m_upgraderResource) && ... )`, in a separate branch for `m_selectedRecipe.Recipe.m_requireOnlyOneIngredient`, and passes `amount` into `SetupRequirement(..., quality, amount)` which does `int num2 = req.GetAmount(quality) * craftMultiplier;` (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1649-1671, 1735-1752). `Piece.Requirement.m_upgraderResource` and `CraftingStation.m_upgrader` are both new in 1.0 (0 hits in the 0.217 decompile; see auga_audit/diffs/Piece.diff '+ public bool m_upgraderResource;').
- **Fix:** Do not re-derive the list. Have PostSetupRequirementList read InventoryGui.instance.m_reqList (the list 1.0 just built) instead of recipe.m_resources, and pass the craft multiplier vanilla used (1, or m_multiCraftAmount when m_multiCrafting) into the amount comparison — or call InventoryGui.SetupRequirement's own per-row result rather than re-running the have/have-not test.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Reading InventoryGui.instance.m_reqList is right, but note the postfix also has to pick up the multiplier: 1.0's signature is `SetupRequirementList(int quality, Player player, bool allowedQuality, int amount)` and Auga's patch (PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:265-276) declares only `quality, player, allowedQuality`. Add `int amount` to the postfix parameter list and pass it into the comparison.

### [auga-lib-5] ChatWindowController, GuiInputFieldSubmit and AugaCompendiumController read UnityEngine.Input, which 1.0 no longer supports

- **Verdict:** partly (reviewer confidence 82)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/ChatWindowController.cs:23-24; AugaUnityLib/GuiInputFieldSubmit.cs:17 (wired at Auga/Minimap_Setup.cs:20); AugaUnityLib/AugaCompendiumController.cs:153
- **Vanilla 1.0:** ZInput.GetKey/GetKeyDown/TryGetKeyStateLowLevel (decomp_assembly_utils/ZInput.cs:2093-2134); Chat.Update (decomp/Chat.cs:217)
- **Trigger:** Click or press Escape while the Auga chat input is open; press Enter to confirm a map-pin name; press Escape to close the Auga compendium/trophy screen.
- **Consequence:** All three shortcuts are dead. Chat never de-focuses on left-click/right-click/Escape (only the gamepad branch still works, since it uses ZInput). A map pin can be placed but its name can never be committed with Enter — GuiInputFieldSubmit.Update also re-calls ActivateInputField() every frame, so the field stays stuck open. Escape does not close the compendium (JoyMenu still does). If the build ever runs with Active Input Handling that raises on legacy reads, these become a per-frame InvalidOperationException out of Update instead.
- **Evidence:** Auga still uses the 0.217 form: `if (Input.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Mouse0) || Input.GetKey(KeyCode.Mouse1) || Input.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Escape) || ZInput.GetButtonDown("JoyButtonB") ...` (ChatWindowController.cs:23-26) — 0.217 vanilla Chat had exactly that line with legacy Input (decomp_old_assembly_valheim/Chat.cs:209), and 1.0 rewrote it to `ZInput.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Mouse0) || ZInput.GetKey(KeyCode.Mouse1) || ZInput.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Escape) || ...` (decomp/Chat.cs:217). A regex sweep for standalone `Input.(GetKey|GetMouse|mousePosition|GetAxis)` over the 0.217 decompiles hits Chat, GameCamera, JoinCode, KeyHints, ManageSavesMenu, MusicMan, PlayerCustomizaton, Settings, RadialConfigHelper and ZInput; over the 1.0 decompiles it hits only the unused decomp_assembly_utils/InputDefinition.cs. 1.0 routes every key read through `ZInput.TryGetKeyStateLowLevel`, which reads `Keyboard.current[KeyCodeToKey(keyCode)]` / `Mouse.current`. Auga's chat copy is live in the runs: 'PortDiagnostics] Chat: 0 destroyed, 1 unassigned UI references / m_search' in auga_run1.log, and no InvalidOperationException appears in any of auga_run1..4.log, so on this build the reads return false silently rather than throwing.
- **Fix:** Replace every `Input.GetKey*` with `ZInput.GetKey*` (same KeyCode overloads exist: ZInput.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Mouse0) etc.). For GuiInputFieldSubmit the better fix is to drop the polling entirely and subscribe to the field's own event, the way ChatWindowController already does: `m_field.OnInputSubmit.AddListener(s => m_onSubmit(s))` (GUIFramework.GuiInputField.OnInputSubmit, decomp_gui_framework/GUIFramework/GuiInputField.cs:26,82,394).
- **Verifier correction:** The three call sites and the vanilla migration are exactly as described, but 'all three shortcuts are dead' is not established — it depends on the game's Active Input Handling setting, which is not visible in any static source here, and the finding's own evidence argues against its conclusion. If the legacy backend were disabled, UnityEngine.Input.GetKeyDown would raise InvalidOperationException, and ChatWindowController.Update calls it unconditionally on its first line every frame with Auga chat enabled by default (Auga.cs:507, default true). I grepped all four run logs for InvalidOperationException and found none — which is equally consistent with legacy input still being enabled ('Both'), in which case the chat de-focus and compendium Escape still work and only the ZInput-vs-Input inconsistency remains. So 'the reads return false silently' is an inference, not a verified fact; the correct status is cannot-verify-at-runtime for the consequence, confirmed for the code defect.
- **Verifier's better fix:** The fix is right either way and should be made regardless of the backend question, since ZInput is what the rest of 1.0 reads. The GuiInputFieldSubmit recommendation is the strongest part: dropping the polling also removes the per-frame ActivateInputField() call that keeps the map-pin field permanently focused. Before claiming the shortcuts are dead in the write-up, settle Active Input Handling from a live run (e.g. press Escape with the Auga chat focused, or watch for an InvalidOperationException once the earlier crashes are out of the way).

### [harmony-9] AugaLog SpawnPlayer hook reads PlayerProfile.m_firstSpawn (new in 1.0), so the 'arrival' log entry now fires only once in a character's life

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 82)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\AugaLog_Hooks.cs:8-18 (AugaLog_Hooks.Postfix on Game.SpawnPlayer)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Game.SpawnPlayer / Game.UpdateRespawn (decomp\Game.cs:485, 740-747); PlayerProfile.m_firstSpawn (decomp\PlayerProfile.cs:88)
- **Trigger:** Log into a world with an existing character.
- **Consequence:** AugaMessageLog.AddArrivalLog is never written again after the character's very first spawn; before 1.0 it appeared on every world join. (No exception - the patch still binds even though SpawnPlayer gained a `bool spawnValkyrie` parameter, because it is patched by name with only one overload.)
- **Evidence:** OLD Game.cs has no `m_playerProfile.m_firstSpawn` at all and decomp_old_assembly_valheim\PlayerProfile.cs contains no m_firstSpawn; the per-session flag was `Game.m_firstSpawn` (decomp_old_assembly_valheim\Game.cs:75, cleared at :618-620). 1.0 added `public bool m_firstSpawn = true;` to PlayerProfile (persisted - PlayerProfile.cs:321 `zPackage.Write(m_firstSpawn)`, :516 `m_firstSpawn = zPackage.ReadBool()`), and Game.UpdateRespawn does `SpawnPlayer(point, m_playerProfile.m_firstSpawn && m_inIntro); ... m_playerProfile.m_firstSpawn = false;`. Auga tests `__instance.m_playerProfile.m_firstSpawn`.
- **Fix:** Use the per-session flag instead: capture `Game.instance.m_firstSpawn` in a prefix of Game.UpdateRespawn (it is still cleared after SpawnPlayer returns, so a SpawnPlayer postfix reading `__instance.m_firstSpawn` restores the old behaviour).

### [crafting-4] Auga's requirement wireframe ignores the new multi-craft multiplier (SetupRequirementList's added `int amount` parameter)

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 80)
- **Auga:** Auga/PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:267-277 (the SetupRequirementList postfix declares only quality/player/allowedQuality) and AugaUnityLib/AugaCraftingPanel.cs:205-218 + 249-252 (GetAmount(quality) with no multiplier)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.SetupRequirementList(int quality, Player player, bool allowedQuality, int amount) (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1642, called at :1544 with num3 = m_multiCraftAmount) and InventoryGui.SetupRequirement(..., int craftMultiplier = 1) (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1736, 1752)
- **Trigger:** Hold AltPlace (or the left stick) over the crafting panel so 1.0 switches the panel into its x5 multi-craft preview.
- **Consequence:** Vanilla multiplies both the displayed requirement amount (`num2 = req.GetAmount(quality) * craftMultiplier`) and the craft-button check (`player.HaveRequirements(recipe, false, num, m_multiCraftAmount)`) by 5. The requirement rows inside Auga's panel are drawn by vanilla and do show the x5 amounts, and the Craft button correctly greys out, but Auga's wireframe still evaluates GetAmount(quality) at x1 and paints every wire 'Have' - the wireframe claims the craft is possible while the button beside it is disabled.
- **Evidence:** Signature change in auga_audit/diffs/InventoryGui.diff: `-private void SetupRequirementList(int quality, Player player, bool allowedQuality)` / `+private void SetupRequirementList(int quality, Player player, bool allowedQuality, int amount)` and `-SetupRequirement(..., craft: true, quality)` / `+SetupRequirement(..., craft: true, quality, amount)`. Auga postfix: `public static void Postfix(InventoryGui __instance, int quality, Player player, bool allowedQuality)` - `amount` is never injected. Auga helper: `var amount = resource.GetAmount(qualityLevel); var num = player.m_inventory.CountItems(resource.m_resItem.m_itemData.m_shared.m_name); if (num < amount) return false;`
- **Fix:** Add `int amount` to the Harmony postfix parameter list (Harmony injects by name) and thread it through PostSetupRequirementList into HaveRequirementsHelper and the wire-state loop as a multiplier, exactly as vanilla's craftMultiplier.

### [pause-texts-5] Menu.m_cloudStorageWarning / m_cloudStorageWarningNextSave resolve to children of the inactive donor, so the cloud-storage dialogs can never be shown or acknowledged

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 80) - crash already prevented by PortCarryOver
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnity\Assets\Prefabs\AugaMenu.prefab:444-445 (`m_cloudStorageWarning: {fileID: 0}`, `m_cloudStorageWarningNextSave: {fileID: 0}`), filled by Auga\PortCarryOver.cs:104-110
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Menu.ShowCloudStorageFullWarning() / OnCloudStorageFullWarningOk() / ShowCloudStorageLowNextSaveWarning() (decomp\Menu.cs:585, :599, :611), reached from OnManualSave, OnQuitYes and OnLogoutYes
- **Trigger:** Manual Save / Exit / Logout from the pause menu while Steam Cloud (or console) save storage is full - i.e. whenever `SaveSystem.CanSaveToCloudStorage(...)` returns false.
- **Consequence:** `m_cloudStorageWarning.SetActive(true)` activates `CloudStorageFullMovingSave` inside the donor, whose root was SetActive(false) - nothing renders. The callback list is filled but OnCloudStorageFullWarningOk can never be clicked, so the save/quit/logout the player asked for silently never happens and there is no on-screen explanation.
- **Evidence:** auga_run4.log:305 lists `m_cloudStorageWarning, m_cloudStorageWarningNextSave` among the 11 donor-filled references. dumps\Menu_vanilla.txt:90 and :99 show `CloudStorageLowNextSave (inactive)` and `CloudStorageFullMovingSave (inactive)` as direct children of the Menu object, which PortCarryOver.cs:23-29 (`original.SetActive(false)`) deactivates. decomp\Menu.cs:596 `m_cloudStorageWarning.SetActive(value: true);` with no visibility fallback.
- **Fix:** Reparent `CloudStorageFullMovingSave` and `CloudStorageLowNextSave` out of the donor onto the Auga menu root in Menu_Start_Patch.Postfix before calling Fill(), or generally: after PortCarryOver.Fill, walk the filled GameObject/Component references and reparent any whose root is the (inactive) donor.

### [inventory-4] m_uiGroups[2] and m_uiGroups[3] are the same UIGroupHandler, so SetActiveGroup(m_uiGroups[3]) deactivates the right panel instead of activating it

- **Verdict:** partly (reviewer confidence 78)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:132-139 (rightPanel.GetComponent<UIGroupHandler>() supplied for both index 2 and index 3)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.SetActiveGroup — scratchpad\decomp\InventoryGui.cs:622-645; callers at InventoryGui.cs:889, 1390, 2061, 2111, 2341-2367, 2563, 2571; gamepad gate at InventoryGui.cs:615-618
- **Trigger:** Any click on a recipe, the craft button, the repair button, a grid slot, or the skills/texts/trophies/PVP buttons — every one of these routes through SetActiveGroup.
- **Consequence:** No exception (the run3 IndexOutOfRangeException at InventoryGui.OnTabCraftPressed is indeed fixed), but the right panel's UIGroupHandler ends up with m_userActive = false exactly when it should be active: the loop `for (i...) m_uiGroups[i].SetActive(i == m_activeGroup)` writes true at i=2 and then false at i=3 on the same object. Consequences: UIGroupHandler.IsActive stays false for the right panel, so its gamepad default-element focus and m_enableWhenActiveAndGamepad never engage; and because SetActiveGroup(UIGroupHandler) resolves through Array.IndexOf it always yields 2, so m_activeGroup can never become 3 and `if (m_activeGroup == 3) UpdateRecipeGamepadInput();` never runs — gamepad crafting navigation is dead. Mouse clicking still works: none of PlayerPanel/ContainerPanel/RightPanel has a CanvasGroup, so UIGroupHandler.Update's `m_canvasGroup.interactable = flag` line is skipped and no Selectable is disabled.
- **Evidence:** PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:137-138 puts `rightPanel.GetComponent<UIGroupHandler>()` in slots 2 and 3 of the same array. `SetActiveGroup(UIGroupHandler group) => SetActiveGroup(Array.IndexOf(m_uiGroups, group), playSound)` (InventoryGui.cs:622-625) returns the FIRST index, i.e. 2. `UIGroupHandler.SetActive(bool active) { m_userActive = active; ... }` (decomp_assembly_guiutils\UIGroupHandler.cs:196-203) and `IsHighestPriority()` returns false when !m_userActive (line 78-80), which drives `IsActive` in Update (line 111-119). Root component dumps: RightPanel.prefab root = [RectTransform, 3x Unity.Auga MB, UIGroupHandler(-626263950)] — no CanvasGroup; same for PlayerPanel.prefab and ContainerPanel.prefab. Vanilla has four distinct handlers (auga_audit\dumps\InventoryGui_vanilla.txt: root/Player/Container, root/Player, root/Info, root/Crafting each carry their own UIGroupHandler).
- **Fix:** Give index 3 its own UIGroupHandler instead of reusing the panel's. E.g. add a UIGroupHandler component to the crafting sub-object inside the right panel (CraftingPanel.gameObject) and use that for m_uiGroups[3], keeping rightPanel's own handler at index 2. If separating them is not practical, at minimum handle the loop ordering so the shared handler is not switched off — but Array.IndexOf will still make m_activeGroup==3 unreachable, so a distinct handler is the real fix.
- **Verifier correction:** The duplicate-handler defect is real, but `m_activeGroup` CAN reach 3 and `UpdateRecipeGamepadInput()` is NOT dead. decomp/InventoryGui.cs:605-618 (UpdateGamepad) calls the int overload directly: `if (ZInput.GetButtonDown("JoyTabRight")) SetActiveGroup(m_activeGroup + 1);` — from group 2 that yields index 3. And at index 3 the loop's last write is `m_uiGroups[3].SetActive(true)`, so the shared handler ends up ACTIVE there. What is actually broken is the reverse of what the finding says for the click paths: every `SetActiveGroup(m_uiGroups[3])` / `SetActiveGroup(m_uiGroups[2])` click resolves to index 2 via Array.IndexOf, and at index 2 the loop writes true at i=2 then false at i=3 on the same object, so the right panel's handler ends INACTIVE on every recipe/craft/repair/tab/skills/texts/trophies/PVP click. Gamepad tabbing still reaches 3 and works.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Same as proposed (a distinct UIGroupHandler for slot 3, e.g. added to the crafting sub-object), but state the reason correctly: with one shared component the loop's last write wins, so index 2 (the info/skills/texts/PVP group) is the one that ends up inactive, not index 3.

### [inventory-6] InventoryGui.m_touchSplitAnchor (new in 1.0) points at root/Player/TouchSplitAnchor, which Auga turns into a hidden donor

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 78) - crash already prevented by PortCarryOver
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:40 (`__instance.Replace("root/Player", Auga.Assets.InventoryScreen, "root/Player")`) via Extensions.cs:89 PortCarryOver.MakeDonor
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.UpdateItemDrag — scratchpad\decomp\InventoryGui.cs:790-795 (`if (IsSplitDropping) m_dragGo.transform.position = m_touchSplitAnchor.position;`)
- **Trigger:** Touch input only: split a stack (long-press / shift equivalent) and then carry the split stack, which sets IsSplitDropping = true.
- **Consequence:** No exception — the donor's transform is still alive — but the dragged split stack is positioned at the anchor inside the hidden, differently-laid-out 'Player_VanillaDonor' branch rather than anywhere in Auga's layout, so it renders in the wrong place. Exactly the README's 'field filled with a HIDDEN donor object where the player actually needs to see it' gap, and PortDiagnostics reports it as fine ('InventoryGui: 0 destroyed, 0 unassigned UI references', auga_run4.log:245) because the reference is assigned.
- **Evidence:** `public Transform m_touchSplitAnchor;` is new in 1.0 (auga_field_diff.csv InventoryGui New column lists m_touchSplitAnchor). auga_audit\dumps\InventoryGui_vanilla.txt line 14: `root/Player/TouchSplitAnchor  []` — it lives inside root/Player, the exact branch Auga replaces. PortCarryOver.MakeDonor only does SetActive(false) + rename + SetAsLastSibling (PortCarryOver.cs:23-29), so the transform survives with its old coordinates and the vanilla InventoryGui field keeps pointing at it.
- **Fix:** After the Replace, re-point it into the Auga panel: `__instance.m_touchSplitAnchor = playerInventory.Find("TouchSplitAnchor")` — adding an empty RectTransform named TouchSplitAnchor to PlayerPanel.prefab at the position Auga wants the split stack to sit. Low priority if touch input is not a target platform, but it is a one-line assignment.

### [crafting-3] AugaCraftingPanel.HaveRequirementsHelper / PostSetupRequirementList ignore Piece.Requirement.m_upgraderResource, so the requirement wireframe disagrees with the craft button

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 72)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/AugaCraftingPanel.cs:205-218 (HaveRequirementsHelper) and 245-258 (the `foreach (var resource in recipe.m_resources)` loop in PostSetupRequirementList)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.SetupRequirementList (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1649-1671) and Player.HaveRequirementItems (decomp/Player.cs:2744-2753)
- **Trigger:** Open the crafting or upgrade tab at a normal workbench/forge on any recipe whose m_resources contains an entry with m_upgraderResource = true (the new 1.0 upgrade-station ingredient).
- **Consequence:** 1.0 filters requirements by station: an entry only counts when `currentCraftingStation.m_upgrader == requirement.m_upgraderResource`, and upgrader resources are skipped entirely when there is no station. Auga's wireframe loop applies no such filter - it walks every m_resources entry with `m_resItem != null && GetAmount(quality) > 0`, and the new Piece.Requirement.GetAmount adds `+ m_amount` for upgrader resources so those always return a positive amount. Auga therefore draws an extra wire and sets canCraft = false for a recipe vanilla considers craftable: the wireframe says 'you cannot make this' while m_craftButton.interactable is true. The same mismatch shifts wire N off the requirement row N that vanilla drew from its filtered m_reqList.
- **Evidence:** 1.0 SetupRequirementList: `if ((!(currentCraftingStation != null) || currentCraftingStation.m_upgrader == requirement2.m_upgraderResource) && (!(currentCraftingStation == null) || !requirement2.m_upgraderResource) && requirement2.GetAmount(quality) > 0) { m_reqList.Add(requirement2); }`. 1.0 Piece.Requirement (auga_audit/diffs/Piece.diff): `public bool m_upgraderResource;` and `return (int)Math.Floor(num * (float)m_amountPerLevel + (float)(m_upgraderResource ? m_amount : 0));`. Auga: `foreach (var resource in recipe.m_resources) { var amountRequired = resource.GetAmount(quality); if (resource.m_resItem != null && amountRequired > 0) { var have = HaveRequirementsHelper(player, new[] { resource }, quality); ... canCraft = canCraft && have; ++index; } }`. Grep of the Auga tree for m_upgraderResource: no hits.
- **Fix:** Mirror vanilla's filter in PostSetupRequirementList - skip a requirement when `currentCraftingStation != null && currentCraftingStation.m_upgrader != resource.m_upgraderResource`, and when `currentCraftingStation == null && resource.m_upgraderResource`. Better: read `InventoryGui.m_reqList`, which vanilla has just populated in the method being postfixed, instead of re-deriving the list from recipe.m_resources.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix is correct, and the second option (read `__instance.m_reqList`) is strictly better — it also picks up the `m_requireOnlyOneIngredient` + IsKnownMaterial filtering in the first branch at decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1651-1657, which the proposed hand-written filter would still miss. One caveat: whether any shipped recipe actually sets m_upgraderResource is game data I cannot read statically; the code-level mismatch is certain.

### [pause-texts-6] Menu.m_startScene (new SceneReference struct) cannot be carried over, so OnCloudStorageLowNextSaveWarningOk has no scene to load

- **Verdict:** partly (reviewer confidence 72)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\PortCarryOver.cs:104-119 (only UnityEngine.Object fields and Object collections are filled); AugaMenu.prefab has no `m_startScene` key
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Menu.OnCloudStorageLowNextSaveWarningOk() (decomp\Menu.cs:619-622) -> SystemResourceManager.FastLoadScene(m_startScene)
- **Trigger:** Cloud storage runs low during an autosave (SaveFinished -> ShowCloudStorageLowNextSaveWarning) and the player presses OK on the resulting dialog.
- **Consequence:** `m_startScene` is a default-constructed `SoftReferenceableAssets.SceneManagement.SceneReference`, so FastLoadScene gets an empty reference - the return to the start scene fails instead of loading "start" (0.217 hard-coded `SceneManager.LoadScene("start")`, which needed no serialized data).
- **Evidence:** decomp\Menu.cs:82 `public SceneReference m_startScene;` (new in 1.0; Menu.diff line 62). auga_field_diff.csv Menu `New` column ends with `m_startScene`. decomp_old_assembly_valheim\Menu.cs used `SceneManager.LoadScene("start")` (Menu.diff line 648). PortCarryOver.FillComponent only handles `typeof(Object).IsAssignableFrom(field.FieldType)` (PortCarryOver.cs:104) and List/array of Object (PortCarryOver.cs:112) - a struct field is skipped silently and is not reported in the diagnostics list.
- **Fix:** In Menu_Start_Patch.Postfix copy the value explicitly alongside CurrentPlayersPrefab: `newMenu.m_startScene = donor.GetComponent<Menu>().m_startScene;`. More generally, extend PortCarryOver to copy non-Object serialized fields that are still at their type default when the donor's differs.
- **Verifier correction:** The mechanism is confirmed, but the stated trigger cannot occur under Auga, so nothing actually reaches FastLoadScene. m_cloudStorageWarningNextSave is one of the 11 fields PortCarryOver filled from the donor (auga_run4.log Menu fill list), and the donor is the whole vanilla `Menu` GameObject — dumps/Menu_vanilla.txt shows CloudStorageLowNextSave and its Dialog/ButtonOk (lines 90-98) as children of that GameObject, which MakeDonor sets inactive. ShowCloudStorageLowNextSaveWarning's `m_cloudStorageWarningNextSave.SetActive(true)` therefore activates an object inside an inactive branch: the dialog is invisible and its OK button unclickable, so OnCloudStorageLowNextSaveWarningOk is unreachable. The real finding is that the warning UI is stranded in the donor; the empty m_startScene is a second-order consequence behind it. I also could not confirm SceneReference is a struct — it lives in SoftReferenceableAssets.dll, which is not in the decompile set — but it is a non-UnityEngine.Object serialized type either way, which is all the argument needs.
- **Verifier's better fix:** The explicit copy in Menu_Start_Patch.Postfix is fine, but fix the reachability first: either re-parent `CloudStorageLowNextSave` / `CloudStorageFullMovingSave` out of the donor into the live Auga menu, or give AugaMenu.prefab its own copies and assign m_cloudStorageWarning/m_cloudStorageWarningNextSave to them — otherwise the m_startScene copy is unreachable code.

### [widgets-2] 1.0 UITooltip.OnHoverStart reuses the existing tooltip instance instead of destroying it, so Auga's two different m_tooltipPrefabs (SimpleTooltip vs InventoryTooltip) get crossed

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 72)
- **Auga:** Auga's 36 UITooltip components across AugaUnity\Assets\Prefabs: 24 prefabs carry `m_tooltipPrefab`, split between guid `8cf9d8fdddc80624189cfefd17cc2646` (InventoryTooltip, e.g. Prefabs\InventoryElement.prefab:379, SkillsElement.prefab:585, ActiveEffectItem.prefab:225, FoodPanel.prefab:1192, AugaStoreScreen.prefab:334) and guid `6bc03576622362b478e8f19c156074f1` (SimpleTooltip, e.g. Prefabs\ButtonMedium.prefab:66, CraftingRequirementElement.prefab:262, RightPanel.prefab:2687). Same split is set at runtime in Auga\API.cs:316 (`Auga.Assets.SimpleTooltip`) vs API.cs:330/352/374/396 (`Auga.Assets.InventoryTooltip`). Patch that has to cope: Auga\UITooltip_Patch.cs:12-49.
- **Vanilla 1.0:** UITooltip.OnHoverStart and UITooltip.LateUpdate, scratchpad\decomp_assembly_guiutils\UITooltip.cs (see auga_audit\diffs\UITooltip.diff)
- **Trigger:** Gamepad/controller play: moving focus between an Auga item slot (InventoryTooltip) and any plain Auga button or crafting-requirement icon (SimpleTooltip). The gamepad path in `LateUpdate` calls `OnHoverStart(m_gamepadFocusObject)` directly with no `OnPointerExit`, so nothing hides the previous tooltip first. Also reachable with a mouse whenever two tooltip elements overlap so no exit event fires between them.
- **Consequence:** The tooltip GameObject built from the *previous* element's prefab is reused. Item -> button leaves a ComplexTooltip in place: Auga's prefix finds no ItemTooltip/FoodTooltip/StatusTooltip/SkillTooltip, calls `customTooltip.SetDefault(__instance)` and then returns `true`, so vanilla `UpdateTextElements` also runs and `Utils.FindChild(m_tooltip.transform, "Text")` (depth-first) lands on `Tooltip/TextBoxContainer/TextBox/Text` - the inactive TwoColumnTextBoxPrefab template - and writes the raw tooltip text into it; every two-column stat box cloned from that template afterwards starts with a stray leading line. Button -> item leaves a SimpleTooltip in place: `GetComponent<ComplexTooltip>()` is null, the prefix returns true, and the item shows as plain topic/text with no icon, no quality, no stat table.
- **Evidence:** OLD (decomp_old_assembly_guiutils\UITooltip.cs): `if (Object.op_Implicit((Object)(object)m_current)) { HideTooltip(); } if ((Object)(object)m_tooltip == (Object)null && (m_text != "" || m_topic != "")) { m_tooltip = Object.Instantiate<GameObject>(m_tooltipPrefab, ...); ... }`.
