- **Evidence:** 1.0 added `public KeyHintsRadial m_radialKeyHints;` (decomp\KeyHints.cs:54) and removed m_radialBackHint/m_radialInteract/m_radialBack - matches auga_field_diff.csv row "KeyHints","22","22","3","3","m_radialBackHint, m_radialInteract, m_radialBack","m_buildMenuHintsKB, m_buildMenuHintsGP, m_radialKeyHints". auga_run4.log line 253 confirms the donor fill: `[PortDiagnostics] KeyHints on 'KeyHints(Clone)': 5 empty references filled from the hidden vanilla object: m_equipButtons[], m_buildMenuHintsKB[], m_buildMenuHintsGP[], m_cycleSnapKey, m_radialKeyHints`. dumps\Hud_vanilla.txt line 518 shows where it lives: `hudroot/KeyHints/RadialHints  (inactive)  [UIInputHint, KeyHintsRadial]`. auga_run1.log line 497 shows the pre-carryover crash this fill prevents: `KeyHints.SetGamePadBindings () ... KeyHints.Awake ()` NRE, i.e. the m_radialKeyHints.UpdateGamepadHints() call at KeyHints.cs:109.
- **Fix:** Add a RadialHints child with a KeyHintsRadial component (its six TextMeshProUGUI and six GameObject slots: interact, back, drop, dropMulti, close, closeTopLevel) to Auga's HUD.prefab KeyHints branch and wire both m_radialHints and m_radialKeyHints to it. Cheap stopgap: after the Replace at Hud_Setup.cs:201, reparent the donor's "RadialHints" transform onto Auga's KeyHints object and set keyHints.GetComponent<KeyHints>().m_radialHints to it, so the object m_radialKeyHints points at is in an active hierarchy.
- **Verifier correction:** The mechanism and the net effect are right, but the stated reason is not. m_radialBackHint/m_radialInteract/m_radialBack no longer exist as fields on 1.0's KeyHints at all — auga_field_diff.csv lists them under Dropped — so their `{fileID: 0}` values in HUD.prefab are inert leftover YAML keys, not the cause of anything. And Auga's RadialHints object is not empty: GameObject &6032538681940285978 carries a UIInputHint (script 556256886, guid d920e4723ba2566674efb2f0c3eb6edb) with m_gamepadHint/m_mouseKeyboardHint pointing at a `Keyboard` child (active) and a `Gamepad` child (inactive) holding a single stale `Text - Look` label. So KeyHints.SetActive(true) shows Auga's 2023 static hint strip, not nothing. What is genuinely lost is 1.0's dynamic hint set: KeyHintsRadial.UpdateRadialHints toggles m_gamepadCloseTopLevel/m_kbCloseTopLevel/m_gamepadClose/m_kbClose/m_gamepadBack/m_kbBack/m_gamepadDrop/m_gamepadDropMulti/m_kbDrop/m_kbDropMulti (decomp/KeyHintsRadial.cs:71-84), and it never runs.
- **Verifier's better fix:** The proper fix is right. The cheap stopgap is not safe as written: KeyHintsRadial.UpdateRadialHints dereferences m_gamepadCloseTopLevel/m_kbCloseTopLevel/m_gamepadClose/m_kbClose/m_gamepadBack/m_kbBack and the four drop fields with no null test, so merely re-parenting the donor's RadialHints into an active hierarchy turns a silent no-op into a per-frame NRE unless those ten slots are populated.

### [widgets-3] PortCarryOver never reaches the shared widgets (all on children, none on donor roots), so every value-type field 1.0 added to UIGroupHandler / TabHandler / UIGamePad / UIInputHint / ButtonSfx stays at its C# default on Auga's 2023 prefabs

- **Verdict:** partly (reviewer confidence 80)
- **Auga:** Auga\PortCarryOver.cs:62-82 (`foreach (var donorComponent in donor.GetComponents<MonoBehaviour>())` - donor ROOT only) and :104-120 (only `typeof(Object).IsAssignableFrom(field.FieldType)` and Object collections are copied). Affected Auga prefab components per auga_audit\auga_script_refs.csv: UIGroupHandler x32, UIGamePad x61, ButtonSfx x51, UITooltip x36, GuiBar x28, UIInputHint x7, UIInputHandler x7 - all on child objects, never on a replaced branch's root.
- **Vanilla 1.0:** UIGroupHandler.Update / SetActive (decomp_assembly_guiutils\UIGroupHandler.cs), UIGamePad.Update / IsBlocked (decomp\UIGamePad.cs), UIInputHint.UpdateInputHints (decomp_assembly_guiutils\UIInputHint.cs), ButtonSfx.OnPointerEnter/PlaySfx (decomp_assembly_guiutils\ButtonSfx.cs), TabHandler.Update (decomp\TabHandler.cs)
- **Trigger:** Always, on every Auga screen; most visible when playing with a gamepad or with gamepad+mouse.
- **Consequence:** 1.0 features that are simply absent under Auga: (a) `m_setDefaultOnKBM=false` on all 32 UIGroupHandlers -> `m_enableWhenActiveAndGamepad` never shows and no default element is auto-selected on keyboard/mouse (`m_enableWhenActiveAndGamepad.SetActive(IsActive && (ZInput.IsExclusiveGamepadActive() || m_setDefaultOnKBM))`); (b) `m_defaultElementFallbackMode = None` -> when the default element is non-interactable 1.0's `NextBelow` walk never runs, so focus lands on a disabled control; (c) `m_availableForConsoleMouse=false` on all 61 UIGamePads -> gamepad button hints vanish and the hotkey is blocked whenever a mouse is also active (`IsBlocked` now returns early unless `m_availableForConsoleMouse || !ZInput.IsGamepadMouseActive()`); (d) `m_enterSfxPrefab`/`m_sfxPrefabVibrationOnly`/`m_selectSfxPrefabVibrationOnly`/`m_enterSfxPrefabVibrationOnly` null on all 51 ButtonSfx -> no hover sound and no controller rumble on any Auga button; (e) `m_gamepadMouseHint` null on all 7 UIInputHint -> no combined gamepad+mouse hint.
- **Evidence:** auga_audit\auga_field_diff.csv: `"UIGroupHandler","4","7","0","3","","m_defaultElementFallbackMode, m_setDefaultOnKBM, m_resetActiveElementOnStateChange"`; `"UIGamePad","5","6","0","1","","m_availableForConsoleMouse"`; `"ButtonSfx","2","6","0","4","","m_sfxPrefabVibrationOnly, m_selectSfxPrefabVibrationOnly, m_enterSfxPrefab, m_enterSfxPrefabVibrationOnly"`; `"UIInputHint","4","5","0","1","","m_gamepadMouseHint"`; `"TabHandler","5","11","0","6",...`. PortCarryOver.FillComponent only assigns when `typeof(Object).IsAssignableFrom(field.FieldType)` or `IsObjectCollection(field.FieldType)`, so bools/enums/strings are skipped by construction, and Fill only enumerates `donor.GetComponents<MonoBehaviour>()` on the root - the run4 log shows it firing only for root components (SkillsDialog, PlayerCustomizaton, KeyHints, Menu), never for a UIGroupHandler/UIGamePad/ButtonSfx.
- **Fix:** Extend PortCarryOver with a per-type table of new 1.0 non-Object defaults applied after Fill, walking `GetComponentsInChildren<T>(true)` on the Auga replacement: set `UIGroupHandler.m_setDefaultOnKBM` and `m_defaultElementFallbackMode` to match the vanilla screen they replace, copy `ButtonSfx.m_enterSfxPrefab` from the vanilla `ButtonSfx` on the donor subtree (match by name path rather than root), and set `UIGamePad.m_availableForConsoleMouse = true` on Auga's UIGamePads so hints/hotkeys survive gamepad+mouse. Also widen Fill to match donor children by relative transform path instead of only the root.
- **Verifier correction:** The substantive claim is right — FillComponent assigns only when `typeof(Object).IsAssignableFrom(field.FieldType)` (PortCarryOver.cs:104) or IsObjectCollection (:112), so every bool/enum/string 1.0 added stays at its C# initializer — but the parenthetical 'all on children, none on donor roots' is false. dumps/InventoryGui_vanilla.txt shows `root [CanvasGroup, UIGroupHandler]` (line 2), `root/Player [UIGroupHandler]` (4), `root/Info [UIGroupHandler]` (51) and `root/Crafting [UIGroupHandler]` (75): UIGroupHandler is on the root of three branches Auga replaces. PortCarryOver.Fill also falls back to `target.GetComponentInChildren(type, true)` on the target side (PortCarryOver.cs:76), so it does reach child components when the donor root has the same type. None of that changes the conclusion, because value-type fields are skipped by construction.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix is sound in shape. One caveat before implementing it: the static sources do not show what values vanilla 1.0's own prefabs use for m_setDefaultOnKBM / m_availableForConsoleMouse / m_defaultElementFallbackMode, so 'set them to match the vanilla screen they replace' needs a runtime read of the donor's corresponding component rather than a guessed table — which is exactly what widening Fill to match donor children by relative transform path would give you for free.

### [auga-lib-7] ComplexTooltip's damage box omits the new HitData.DamageTypes.m_nonPlayer channel

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 80)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/ComplexTooltip.cs:564-602 (AddDamageTextbox), the m_damage..m_spirit chain at :579-596
- **Vanilla 1.0:** HitData.DamageTypes.m_nonPlayer and DamageTypes.GetTooltipString (decomp/HitData.cs:289; auga_audit/diffs/HitData.diff)
- **Trigger:** Hover or inspect any 1.0 weapon/ammo that carries non-player damage.
- **Consequence:** The non-player damage component never appears in Auga's damage box, so the weapon reads as weaker than vanilla's tooltip reports it. Vanilla's own string builder (GetDamage(...).GetTooltipString(...)) does print it.
- **Evidence:** auga_audit/diffs/HitData.diff adds `+ public float m_nonPlayer;` to DamageTypes and reworks HaveDamage to `... && !(m_spirit > 0f)) { return m_nonPlayer > 0f; }`. Auga lists exactly nine channels and stops at spirit: `if (damage.m_spirit != 0.0f) AddDamageLine(textBox, item, "$inventory_spirit", damage.m_spirit, previousDamage.m_spirit, min, max, upgrade);` (ComplexTooltip.cs:595-596) with no m_nonPlayer line.
- **Fix:** Add one more AddDamageLine for `damage.m_nonPlayer` (label '$inventory_nonplayer' or whichever key 1.0's DamageTypes.GetTooltipString uses for that channel) after the spirit line, mirroring the existing pattern.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Add the line, but 1.0's own builder prints a hardcoded English label, not a localization key: decomp/HitData.cs:554 is `m_sb.Append($"\nNonPlayer: <color=orange>{Mathf.RoundToInt(m_nonPlayer)}</color> …")` (the neighbouring spirit line uses `$inventory_spirit`). There is no `$inventory_nonplayer` key to reuse — use the literal "NonPlayer" or add a new key.

### [widgets-4] ComplexTooltip has no rows for the tooltip content 1.0 added to ItemDrop.GetTooltip (equipment modifiers, chain/spawn-on-hit, adrenaline, damage multipliers), and its GetExtraTextFromTooltip salvage heuristic only catches them by accident

- **Verdict:** partly (reviewer confidence 74)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib\ComplexTooltip.cs:348-502 `GenerateItemTextBoxes` and :504-539 `GetExtraTextFromTooltip` (the `foundWeight` / `foundFirstColor` line scanner over `_originalTooltip`, set at :230 `_originalTooltip = item.GetTooltip();`)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip (scratchpad\decomp\ItemDrop.cs:848-1048), Player.AppendEquipmentModifierTooltips (decomp\Player.cs:2594-2614), ItemDrop.ItemData.GetChainTooltip (decomp\ItemDrop.cs:1118)
- **Trigger:** Hovering any equipped-gear item in an Auga inventory/crafting tooltip.
- **Consequence:** The new 1.0 per-item stats never get a styled two-column row. `$item_movement_modifier`, `$item_heat_modifier` and `$item_maxadrenaline` start with `$item_` so `GetExtraTextFromTooltip` skips them until some line trips `foundFirstColor`; the `$se_*` stamina modifiers and `$base_item_modifier` do trip it, after which everything left (set effect, adrenaline, chain tooltip) is dumped verbatim - with raw `<color=orange>`/`<color=yellow>` markup and `$item_total:` fragments - into one left-aligned block at the bottom, duplicating the set-effect and status-effect boxes Auga already built. Items whose tooltip never trips the flag (e.g. plain armour with no status effect) show none of it at all.
- **Evidence:** decomp\Player.cs: `s_equipmentModifierTooltips = new string[11] { "$item_movement_modifier", "$base_item_modifier", "$item_heat_modifier", "$se_jumpstamina", "$se_attackstamina", "$se_blockstamina", "$se_dodgestamina", "$se_swimstamina", "$se_sneakstamina", "$se_runstamina", "$item_maxadrenaline" };` emitted by `AppendEquipmentModifierTooltips`, which 1.0 calls at decomp\ItemDrop.cs:1035 - it does not exist in decomp_old_assembly_valheim\ItemDrop.cs. Also new since 0.217 in the same method: `GetChainTooltip` (ItemDrop.cs:1022/1118, `$item_chancetoapplyse`), `$item_fulladrenaline` (:1044), `$item_damagemultipliertotal` / `$item_damagemultiplierhp` (:938/:942), `$item_healthhitreturn` / `$item_eitrhitreturn` (:958/:962), `$item_tamedonly` (:979), `m_shared.m_appendToolTip` recursion (:1047-1048, `grep -c m_appendToolTip` = 0 in the old file, 3 in the new). Auga's scanner: `if ((line.Contains("$item_") || line.Contains("$inventory_")) && !foundWeight) { ... } else { if (foundWeight) { if ((line.StartsWith("<color") || (!line.StartsWith("$item_") && !line.StartsWith("$inventory_"))) && !foundFirstColor) foundFirstColor = true; if (!foundFirstColor) continue; ...` .
- **Fix:** Add explicit rows in `GenerateItemTextBoxes`: call `Player.m_localPlayer.AppendEquipmentModifierTooltips(item, sb)` into a scratch StringBuilder and emit one `TextBoxAddPreprocessedLine(textBox, item, id, value)` per non-zero `s_equipmentModifierTooltips[i]` (reflect `Player.s_equipmentModifierSourceFields` / `GetEquipmentModifierPlusSE(i)`), plus a centred box for `GetChainTooltip` and a line for `$item_fulladrenaline` / `$item_tamedonly` / the two damage multipliers. Then tighten `GetExtraTextFromTooltip` so it stops treating `$se_`/`$base_` lines as the start of the mod-extras region.
- **Verifier correction:** The 1.0 evidence is exactly right and the gap is real, but two claims in the consequence are off. (1) `$item_movement_modifier` is NOT among the lines with no styled row: ComplexTooltip.GenerateItemTextBoxes already emits its own `$item_movement_modifier` row (and an `$item_eitrregen_modifier` row) at roughly ComplexTooltip.cs:470-482, driven off item.m_shared.m_movementModifier — so for that one line the risk is duplication with the salvaged block, not absence. (2) The salvaged block is additionally gated: `if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(extraText) && showExtraText)` at ComplexTooltip.cs:493, with showExtraText cleared at :382/:388, so on those paths the extra text is dropped entirely rather than dumped at the bottom. The genuinely row-less new content is $item_heat_modifier, the seven $se_*stamina entries, $base_item_modifier, $item_maxadrenaline, the chain tooltip, $item_fulladrenaline, $item_tamedonly, the two damage multipliers and the two hit-returns.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Keep the proposed explicit rows, but skip $item_movement_modifier (Auga already renders it) or remove Auga's hand-rolled row in favour of the reflected one so the value isn't printed twice. And when tightening GetExtraTextFromTooltip, also handle the new pre-weight lines — $item_subtitle text and the cheated marker sit BEFORE $item_weight and are discarded by the same scanner (see auga-lib-10), so both findings want one rewrite, not two.

### [minimap-4] Minimap_Setup overwrites m_hints with a single Auga object, so the whole 1.0 large-map key-hint block (including the new gamepadmouse_hints row and the keyboard Ping hint) never shows

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 72)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\Minimap_Setup.cs:133 — `minimap.m_hints = new List<GameObject> { newMap.Find("PingPanel").gameObject };`
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Minimap.SetMapMode, MapMode.Large branch (scratchpad\decomp\Minimap.cs:1238-1242)
- **Trigger:** Open the large map with the "KeyHints" setting on (PlatformPrefs.GetInt("KeyHints", 1) == 1).
- **Consequence:** Only Auga's own single PingPanel hint is toggled. The 1.0 map hint rows — Add pin / Cross off pin / Remove pin / Ping for keyboard, gamepad, and the new gamepad+mouse layout — plus the IconPanel mouse1/mouse2 hints stay inside the inactive large_VanillaDonor and are never shown, so a new player gets no on-screen hint for how to add, cross off, remove or ping a pin.
- **Evidence:** `foreach (GameObject hint in m_hints) { hint.SetActive(active); }` is the only consumer of m_hints. The 1.0 hierarchy has `large/KeyHints [UIInputHint]` with three rows `gamepad_hints`, `keyboard_hints` (AddPin, CrossOffPin, RemovePin, PingPanel) and `gamepadmouse_hints`, plus `large/IconPanel/iconhints [UIInputHint]` (dumps\Minimap_vanilla.txt:49-54, 76-103). Auga's HUD/hudroot/MiniMap/large has no KeyHints and its IconPanel's only children are Icon0..Icon4 — verified by parsing HUD.prefab. `gamepadmouse_hints` is genuinely new: UIInputHint gained `public GameObject m_gamepadMouseHint;` in 1.0 (UIInputHint.diff @@ -16,8 +16,10 @@). m_hints is a List<GameObject>, so PortCarryOver's empty-list fill cannot help — Auga's list is non-empty.
- **Fix:** Append the surviving vanilla hint objects instead of replacing the list, e.g. keep the donor's entries: `minimap.m_hints = new List<GameObject> { newMap.Find("PingPanel").gameObject };` -> build the list from the donor's `large_VanillaDonor/KeyHints` and `large_VanillaDonor/IconPanel/iconhints` re-parented onto Auga's `large`, or author equivalent hint rows in Auga's large prefab and add them all to m_hints.

### [crafting-5] At an upgrader station Auga forces the "max quality" panel and returns early, hiding 1.0's new upgrade-past-max flow

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 70)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/AugaCraftingPanel.cs:220-235 - `if (TabController.SelectedIndex == 1 && item != null) { var maxQuality = item.m_shared.m_maxQuality == item.m_quality; if (maxQuality) { ActivatePanel(MaxQualityUpgradeRequirementsPanel); return; } ... }`
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.UpdateRecipeList (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1180, 1192-1214), InventoryGui.UpdateRecipe (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1481, 1505, 1525-1528, 1569-1572), InventoryGui.DoCrafting (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1841-1893)
- **Trigger:** Stand at a 1.0 crafting station whose CraftingStation.m_upgrader is true, open the Upgrade tab and select an item already at its max quality.
- **Consequence:** 1.0 deliberately lists max-quality items at an upgrader and lets you upgrade them - `canCraft = (tempItem.m_quality < maxQuality || ((flag || globalKey) && tempItem.m_quality >= maxQuality)) && ...`, `flag2 = num <= maxQuality || (currentCraftingStation != null && currentCraftingStation.m_upgrader)`, the craft-type label becomes `$inventory_upgraderwarning`, the button becomes `$inventory_upgraderbutton`, and DoCrafting runs the success / break / level-down gamble. Auga instead swaps to MaxQualityUpgradeRequirementsPanel (the 'cannot go higher' panel) and returns before the wireframe is updated, so the entire new upgrader mechanic is invisible and looks broken even though the vanilla Craft button underneath is live.
- **Evidence:** New in 1.0 (auga_audit/diffs/CraftingStation.diff): `public bool m_upgrader;`, `public EffectList m_craftItemDoneFailEffects`. In InventoryGui: `bool flag = (bool)currentCraftingStation && currentCraftingStation.m_upgrader;` in both UpdateRecipeList and UpdateRecipe, `m_itemCraftType.text = Localization.instance.Localize("$inventory_upgraderwarning")`, `componentInChildren.text = Localization.instance.Localize("$inventory_upgraderbutton")`, and in DoCrafting the `m_shared.m_upgradeChance` / `m_shared.m_breakChance` / `$msg_upgrader_success|broke|failed` branch. Grep of the Auga tree for m_upgrader: no hits.
- **Fix:** Gate the max-quality branch on the station: `var station = player.GetCurrentCraftingStation(); if (maxQuality && !(station != null && station.m_upgrader)) { ActivatePanel(MaxQualityUpgradeRequirementsPanel); return; }`, and give the upgrade panel a state that surfaces $inventory_upgraderwarning and the break/downgrade chance.

### [crafting-9] Touch multi-craft is dead: nothing wires 1.0's new OnCraftPointerDown/Up/Exit to Auga's craft button

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 52)
- **Auga:** Auga/PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:95-98 - only `m_craftButton.onClick.AddListener(__instance.OnCraftPressed)` and the cancel button are wired; no EventTrigger is added in code and no `m_Delegates` (EventTrigger) block exists in AugaUnity/Assets/Prefabs/RightPanel.prefab or TabContent_Crafting.prefab
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.OnCraftPointerDown / OnCraftPointerUp / OnCraftPointerExit (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:2023-2037), TouchLongPressStarted / TouchLongPressPerformed (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:2039-2053), consumed at :1506 and :2065
- **Trigger:** On a touch device, long-press the Craft button to start a x5 multi-craft.
- **Consequence:** 1.0 drives touch multi-craft through `m_currentlyHoldingOnCraftButton`, set only by the three new public pointer callbacks that the vanilla prefab wires through the EventTrigger on its CraftButton. Auga's craft button has no such wiring, so m_currentlyHoldingOnCraftButton stays false, `TouchLongPressPerformed` never sets m_touchMultiCrafting, and the long-press indicator never shows. Keyboard/gamepad multi-craft (holding AltPlace / JoyLStick) still works, since that path is polled directly in UpdateRecipe and OnCraftPressed.
- **Evidence:** dumps/InventoryGui_vanilla.txt: `root/Crafting/Decription/craft_button_panel/CraftButton  [Image, Button, ButtonTextColor, UITooltip, UIGamePad, EventTrigger]` - EventTrigger is present there and appears nowhere in Auga's crafting prefabs. 1.0 code: `private void TouchLongPressPerformed() { if (m_currentlyHoldingOnCraftButton) { m_touchMultiCrafting = true; } }` and `bool flag3 = itemData == null && (ZInput.GetButton("AltPlace") || ZInput.GetButton("JoyLStick") || m_touchMultiCrafting);`. Grep of the Auga tree for CraftPointer, TouchLongPress, m_touchMultiCrafting, m_currentlyHolding: no hits.
- **Fix:** After wiring m_craftButton in PlayerInventory_Setup.cs, add an EventTrigger to `CraftingPanel.CraftButton.gameObject` with PointerDown -> `__instance.OnCraftPointerDown`, PointerUp -> `__instance.OnCraftPointerUp`, PointerExit -> `__instance.OnCraftPointerExit` (all three are public in 1.0). Confirm against the real bundle: the prefab YAML in this repo is out of sync with AssetBundles/augaassets, so absence of an EventTrigger there is suggestive rather than conclusive.

## Severity: cosmetic

### [inventory-7] InventoryGrid_Patches.cs must stay excluded from the build — it is a verbatim re-implementation of the 0.217.30 UpdateGui and no longer compiles against 1.0

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 95)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\InventoryGrid_Patches.cs:12-139; exclusion at Auga\Auga.csproj:50 `<Compile Remove="API.External.cs;InventoryGrid_Patches.cs" />`
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGrid (removed nested class Element, renamed handlers) — scratchpad\auga_audit\diffs\InventoryGrid.diff lines 15-42 and 498-513; scratchpad\decomp\InventoryGrid.cs:419-469
- **Trigger:** Only if a porter re-adds the file to <Compile> in an attempt to fix the UpdateGui crash.
- **Consequence:** Compile errors, and if adapted it would be worse than the current state: the prefix returns false and re-implements the 2023 body, silently dropping everything 1.0 added to UpdateGui — InventoryElement, m_canBeDroppedOn/CanDropDragOntoItem, the drop-focus fader, touch selection, the null-element guard, OnLeftClick/OnLeftUp/OnPointerEnter and the UIDragHandler wiring — which would also re-break the drag handlers InventoryGui expects.
- **Evidence:** The file constructs `new InventoryGrid.Element() { m_pos = ..., m_go = ... }` (lines 48-62); the nested `private class Element` was deleted in 1.0 (InventoryGrid.diff lines 15-42 are all `-`) and `m_elements` is now `List<InventoryElement>`. It also references `__instance.OnRightClick` / `__instance.OnLeftClick` with the 0.217 semantics (lines 41-42); 1.0 renamed those to `OnRightDown` / `OnLeftDown` and added a *different* `OnLeftClick` (decomp\InventoryGrid.cs:419, 430, 450). It calls `__instance.GetElement(x, y, width)` expecting the old Element return type and uses `ZInput.IsGamepadActive()` where 1.0's UpdateGui uses `ZInput.IsExclusiveGamepadActive()`. It is also `namespace Auga;` file-scoped, which the csproj's LangVersion 10 does allow, so the exclusion is the only thing keeping it out.
- **Fix:** Leave `<Compile Remove="...InventoryGrid_Patches.cs" />` in place and do not revive it. Fix the crash through PortInventoryElement (add UIDragHandler) and the Awake postfix (re-attach CanDropDragOntoItem and the other delegates) so vanilla's 1.0 UpdateGui runs intact; consider deleting the file to stop it being mistaken for the fix.

### [hud-build-6] SetupPieceInfo replacement hardcodes "None" and drops the NoCraftCost guard on the crafting-station requirement row

- **Verdict:** partly (reviewer confidence 92)
- **Auga:** Auga/Hud_Setup.cs:409-411 (`component3.text = "None"; component3.color = Mathf.Sin(Time.time * 10f) > 0.0 ? Color.red : Color.white;`)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud.SetupPieceInfo (scratchpad/decomp/Hud.cs:1568-1569)
- **Trigger:** Hovering a build piece that needs a crafting station while no such station is in range.
- **Consequence:** The station requirement row shows an untranslated English "None" in every language, and it keeps flashing red even when the `nocraftcost` global key is set.
- **Evidence:** 1.0 localises the string and gates the flash: `component3.text = Localization.instance.Localize("$menu_none"); component3.color = ((Mathf.Sin(Time.time * 10f) > 0f && !ZoneSystem.instance.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost)) ? Color.red : Color.white);` (decomp/Hud.cs:1568-1569). 0.217.30 had the bare literal `component3.text = "None";` and no key check (decomp_old_assembly_valheim/Hud.cs:1342-1345), which is what Auga's reimplementation still carries.
- **Fix:** Mirror decomp/Hud.cs:1568-1569 in Auga/Hud_Setup.cs:409-411: use `Localization.instance.Localize("$menu_none")` and add `&& !ZoneSystem.instance.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost)` to the red-flash condition.
- **Verifier correction:** The Auga-side defect is real and the fix is right, but the history is wrong: 0.217.30 DID have the NoCraftCost guard. decomp_old_assembly_valheim/Hud.cs:1349-1350 reads `component3.text = "None";` followed by `((Graphic)component3).color = ((Mathf.Sin(Time.time * 10f) > 0f && !ZoneSystem.instance.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost)) ? Color.red : Color.white);`. So only the `$menu_none` localisation is a 1.0 change; dropping the NoCraftCost check is Auga's own pre-existing divergence from 0.217, not something 1.0 added.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix is correct as written (use `$menu_none` and re-add `&& !ZoneSystem.instance.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys.NoCraftCost)`); just drop the claim that the guard is new in 1.0.

### [crafting-7] InventoryGui.m_crafting is never re-pointed at Auga's panel, so 1.0's new SetRecipe group check never matches and the recipe-select feedback is lost

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 88) - crash already prevented by PortCarryOver
- **Auga:** Auga/PlayerInventory_Setup.cs:60-63 - `root/Crafting` becomes a donor via PortCarryOver.MakeDonor and no `__instance.m_crafting = ...` assignment exists anywhere in the Auga tree (only m_craftingStationName/Icon/LevelRoot/Level at :91-94)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.SetRecipe (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1455) - `if (flag && (ActiveGroup == -1 || m_crafting == m_uiGroups[ActiveGroup].transform)) { GamepadRumble.instance.PlayGlobalSelectVibration(); }`
- **Trigger:** Select any recipe (a click, or UpdateCraftingPanel re-selecting after a craft completes).
- **Consequence:** m_crafting still points at the hidden `Crafting_VanillaDonor` RectTransform, which is never equal to `m_uiGroups[ActiveGroup].transform` (Auga's right panel), and ActiveGroup is never -1 after Awake, so the new per-selection gamepad rumble never fires. No exception: PortCarryOver keeps the donor object alive, which is also what keeps `Awake`'s `m_crafting.gameObject.SetActive(true)` (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:367) safe.
- **Evidence:** 1.0 added the comparison - m_crafting appears at decomp/InventoryGui.cs:60 (field), :367 (Awake) and :1455 (new); the 0.217 file has it only at :31 and :296. Auga never assigns it.
- **Fix:** Add `__instance.m_crafting = rightPanel.RectTransform();` (or the transform used as the crafting UI group) alongside the other re-points in PlayerInventory_Setup.cs, so the new group comparison resolves.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix works. Two corrections to the reasoning: m_activeGroup is initialised to 1 (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:50) and SetActiveGroup clamps/wraps into 0..m_uiGroups.Length-1 (:627-641), so ActiveGroup is never -1 at all, not merely "never after Awake". And the donor is irrelevant to Awake's `m_crafting.gameObject.SetActive(true)` at :367 — Harmony postfixes run after the original body, so root/Crafting is still live when that line executes.

### [world-ui-2] StoreGui is the only replaced screen that never goes through PortCarryOver, so m_tooltipAnchor stays null and the screen has no donor safety net

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 85)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\Store_Setup.cs:14-50 (SetupAugaStoreGui / GetAugaStoreGui use a plain Object.Instantiate plus originalTransform.gameObject.SetActive(false), never PortCarryOver.MakeDonor / InstantiateFilled); AugaUnity\Assets\Prefabs\AugaStoreScreen.prefab, StoreGui component &1778101921818948584: `m_coinPrefab: {fileID: 0}` and `m_tooltipAnchor: {fileID: 0}`
- **Vanilla 1.0:** StoreGui.FillList (decomp\StoreGui.cs:278 and :282) passes m_tooltipAnchor to UITooltip.Set(topic, text, anchor); UITooltip.AnchorTooltip (decomp_assembly_guiutils\UITooltip.cs)
- **Trigger:** Opening the trader screen with a gamepad or touch active and moving the selection over an item (UITooltip.AnchorTooltip only runs under ZInput.IsExclusiveGamepadActive()/IsTouchActive()).
- **Consequence:** m_anchor is null, so AnchorTooltip falls through to positioning the tooltip at the midpoint of the hovered row's top edge instead of the panel's tooltip anchor - tooltips drift over the item list. No exception. The structural half matters more: because StoreGui alone skips MakeDonor/Fill, any Object field 1.0 or a future patch adds to StoreGui will be plain null with no donor to fill it, unlike every other replaced screen.
- **Evidence:** auga_run4.log lines 246-247: `[PortDiagnostics] StoreGui: 0 destroyed, 2 unassigned UI references` / `unassigned (may be fine - vanilla leaves some empty): m_coinPrefab, m_tooltipAnchor`. m_coinPrefab is assigned a moment later in GetAugaStoreGui (`newStoreGui.m_coinPrefab = ObjectDB.instance.GetItemPrefab("Coins").GetComponent<ItemDrop>();`) but m_tooltipAnchor is assigned nowhere in Auga. The vanilla object it should point at exists: dumps\StoreGui_vanilla.txt line 34 `Store/TooltipAnchor`. Contrast Barber_Setup.cs:14 and EnemeyHud_Setup.cs:16, which both use SetupHelper.DirectObjectReplace and therefore get PortCarryOver.Fill - that is what filled PlayerCustomizaton's m_noHair/m_noBeard in the same log at line 248.
- **Fix:** Either add a TooltipAnchor RectTransform to AugaStoreScreen.prefab and assign m_tooltipAnchor, or - consistent with the rest of the port - convert Store_Setup to `SetupHelper.DirectObjectReplace(instance.transform, Auga.Assets.StoreGui, "Store_Screen")` in an Awake prefix so PortCarryOver.MakeDonor keeps Store_Screen as a hidden donor and Fill copies m_tooltipAnchor (and anything 1.0 adds later) automatically.

### [harmony-14] Two patches declare __instance with a type unrelated to the patched class (DamageText.Awake / Game._RequestRespawn)

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 85)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\DamageText_Setup.cs:13 (`public static bool Prefix(TextInput __instance)` on DamageText.Awake) and C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\Barber_Setup.cs:21 (`public static void Postfix(PlayerCustomizaton __instance)` on Game._RequestRespawn)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** DamageText.Awake (decomp\DamageText.cs:51) and Game._RequestRespawn (decomp\Game.cs:624)
- **Trigger:** Game start (DamageText.Awake) and every respawn request (Game._RequestRespawn).
- **Consequence:** Harmony emits `ldarg.0` with no cast, so a DamageText is passed where a TextInput is declared and a Game where a PlayerCustomizaton is declared. It works today because the generated method is unverifiable dynamic IL and the parameter is either unused (Barber) or only used for `.transform` (DamageText), but it is wrong by construction and will break the moment either body touches a type-specific member or Harmony adds a type check.
- **Evidence:** DamageText_Setup.DamageText_Awake_Patch is attributed `[HarmonyPatch(typeof(DamageText), nameof(DamageText.Awake))]` and its prefix takes `TextInput __instance` then calls `SetupHelper.DirectObjectReplace(__instance.transform, Auga.Assets.DamageText, "DamageText")`. Barber_Setup.GameRequestRespawn_Awake_Patch is attributed `[HarmonyPatch(typeof(Game), nameof(Game._RequestRespawn))]` with `Postfix(PlayerCustomizaton __instance)`. Both ran without error in auga_run4.log (`[PortDiagnostics] DamageText: 0 destroyed, 0 unassigned`, and the run reaches `Spawned after 8.019993`).
- **Fix:** Change the parameter types to `DamageText __instance` and `Game __instance` (or drop __instance in the Barber postfix, which never uses it).

### [auga-lib-6] BestiaryStatBlock misses 1.0's SlightlyWeak/SlightlyResistant modifiers and emits a bogus row for the new DamageType.NonPlayer

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 84)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/AugaCompendiumController.cs:40-50 (SetStats) and :58-71 (GetDamageTypes)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** HitData.DamageModifier and HitData.DamageType (decomp/HitData.cs; auga_audit/diffs/HitData.diff), HitData.DamageModifiers.GetModifier
- **Trigger:** Open the compendium's Bestiary tab and select a creature.
- **Consequence:** Creatures that are only slightly weak or slightly resistant to a damage type show '-' in the Weakness/Resistance rows, because Auga only queries Weak/VeryWeak/Resistant/VeryResistant/Immune. Separately, `Enum.GetValues(typeof(HitData.DamageType))` now yields the new `NonPlayer = 0x800` member, so a creature with m_nonPlayer set renders an untranslated '$inventory_nonplayer' line in the weakness/resistance/immune list.
- **Evidence:** auga_audit/diffs/HitData.diff: DamageModifier gains `+ SlightlyResistant, + SlightlyWeak` after VeryWeak; DamageType gains `+ Damage = 0x400, + NonPlayer = 0x800`; DamageModifiers gains `+ public DamageModifier m_nonPlayer;` and GetModifier gains `DamageType.NonPlayer => m_nonPlayer`. Auga: `var weak = GetDamageTypes(humanoid, HitData.DamageModifier.Weak); var veryWeak = GetDamageTypes(humanoid, HitData.DamageModifier.VeryWeak);` (AugaCompendiumController.cs:40-46) and `foreach (HitData.DamageType damageType in Enum.GetValues(typeof(HitData.DamageType))) { var mod = humanoid.m_damageModifiers.GetModifier(damageType); ... }` (:61-68). 1.0's own tooltip writer handles the new tiers: SE_Stats.GetDamageModifiersTooltipString has `case HitData.DamageModifier.SlightlyResistant:` and `case HitData.DamageModifier.SlightlyWeak:` (decomp/SE_Stats.cs:718-727).
- **Fix:** Add SlightlyWeak to the allWeak concat and SlightlyResistant to the allResist concat in SetStats, and skip the non-elemental/composite members in GetDamageTypes by filtering the loop: `if (damageType == HitData.DamageType.NonPlayer || damageType == HitData.DamageType.Damage || damageType == HitData.DamageType.Physical || damageType == HitData.DamageType.Elemental) continue;`.

### [auga-lib-9] AugaBindingDisplay's key-name string surgery targets 0.217 KeyCode names; 1.0 returns Input-System display strings

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 83)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/AugaBindingDisplay.cs:51-79 (the Equals/BackQuote switch, the Keypad/Alpha prefix rewrites, and the numpad* Replace calls)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Localization.GetBoundKeyString -> ZInput.GetBoundKeyString (decomp_assembly_guiutils/Localization.cs:471-479, decomp_assembly_utils/ZInput.cs:2142-2173)
- **Trigger:** Any key hint that resolves to a numpad key, '=', '`' or a number row key — visible in the HUD key hints (Auga/Hud_Setup.cs:527,551).
- **Consequence:** The prettifying never fires, so hints show raw Input-System labels: 'Numpad 1' instead of 'Num 1', 'Numpad +' instead of '+', and the '=' / '`' special cases are dead. Because the box choice is `isOneCharLong = localizedKeyString.Length == 1`, multi-word numpad labels also land in LongKeybindBox and overflow the narrow key badge.
- **Evidence:** Auga assumes KeyCode.ToString() output: `case "Equals": localizedKeyString = "="; break; case "BackQuote": ... if (localizedKeyString.StartsWith("Keypad")) localizedKeyString = localizedKeyString.Replace("Keypad", "Num"); else if (localizedKeyString.StartsWith("Alpha")) ...` and `case "numpadPlus": localizedKeyString = localizedKeyString.Replace("Plus", "+");` (AugaBindingDisplay.cs:51-79). 0.217 built the string from `value.m_key` (a KeyCode) — decomp_old_assembly_utils/ZInput.cs:1050-1067 switches on `KeyCode key = value.m_key;`. 1.0 returns either a small localization token or the Input System's own label: `if (!s_keyLocalizationMap.TryGetValue(key, out var value2)) { return inputBinding.ToDisplayString(InputBinding.DisplayStringOptions.DontIncludeInteractions, Keyboard.current); } return value2;` with s_keyLocalizationMap covering only comma/period/mouse buttons/space/shift/alt/ctrl/enter (decomp_assembly_utils/ZInput.cs:620-638, 2165-2173).
- **Fix:** Drive the display off the binding path, which the port already resolves correctly in GetBoundControl, rather than off the localized string: map `control` ('numpad1'..'numpad9', 'numpadPlus', 'equals', 'backquote', 'digit1'...) to the short label and only fall back to Localization.GetBoundKeyString when the control is unrecognised. Delete the Keypad/Alpha prefix rewrites and the Replace('Plus','+') family.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix is right, and GetBoundControl (AugaBindingDisplay.cs:84+) already returns the path's last segment, so the mapping table is a small change. Note that for '=' , '`' and the digit row the Input System's own display names already produce the wanted glyph, so those special cases are merely dead rather than producing wrong output — the visible damage is confined to the numpad labels and the resulting LongKeybindBox selection.

### [text-chat-8] DamageText.Awake prefix declares __instance as TextInput instead of DamageText

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 82)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\DamageText_Setup.cs:14 - `public static bool Prefix(TextInput __instance)` inside `[HarmonyPatch(typeof(DamageText), nameof(DamageText.Awake))]`
- **Vanilla 1.0:** DamageText.Awake (scratchpad\decomp\DamageText.cs:49-53)
- **Trigger:** Plugin patching / DamageText.Awake at world load.
- **Consequence:** Harmony emits a bare `ldarg.0` for __instance with no cast, so a DamageText instance is handed to a parameter typed TextInput. It happens to work today because the body only reads `__instance.transform` (a Component member), but any future use of a TextInput member on that reference is type-confused, and a stricter Harmony build would reject or mis-cast the patch.
- **Evidence:** The neighbouring TextInput_Setup.cs:11 has the identical line `public static bool Prefix(TextInput __instance)` - the DamageText copy was clearly duplicated from it and the type was never changed. The body is `return !SetupHelper.DirectObjectReplace(__instance.transform, Auga.Assets.DamageText, "DamageText");`. auga_run4.log line 251 shows the patch does run (`[PortDiagnostics] DamageText: 0 destroyed, 0 unassigned UI references`) with no exception.
- **Fix:** Change the parameter to `public static bool Prefix(DamageText __instance)`.

### [text-chat-3] Auga's NpcDialog and NpcDialogLarge templates are serialized active while vanilla 1.0's are inactive, and Chat.Awake only deactivates m_worldTextBase

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 80)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnity\Assets\Prefabs\AugaChat.prefab - GameObject &6571844204300040975 "NpcDialog" has `m_IsActive: 1` (RectTransform line 1036, anchoredPosition 960/535.7, anchor 0,0) and GameObject &6572251113943847494 "NpcDialogLarge" has `m_IsActive: 1` (RectTransform line 1108, anchoredPosition 960/222.3); both are referenced by the Chat component at AugaChat.prefab lines 1420-1421 as m_npcTextBase / m_npcTextBaseLarge
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Chat.Awake (scratchpad\decomp\Chat.cs, `m_input.gameObject.SetActive(value: false); m_worldTextBase.SetActive(value: false);`) - it never deactivates the two NPC text templates because the vanilla prefab ships them inactive
- **Trigger:** Entering the world (the frame the Auga chat object is activated).
- **Consequence:** Both template dialog boxes are live in the hierarchy. NpcDialog.controller's default state is `npc_dialog_show` (m_Tag: visible) which auto-advances to `npc_dialog_visible`, so two empty dialog frames flash near the middle of the screen before the `visible == false` condition drives them to npc_dialog_hide/hidden. They then sit there invisible but present forever.
- **Evidence:** dumps\Chat_vanilla.txt lines 4 and 8: `NpcDialog  (inactive)  [CanvasGroup, Animator]` and `NpcDialogLarge  (inactive)  [CanvasGroup, Animator]`. Auga prefab dump: `AugaChat/AugaChat/NpcDialog act=1` and `AugaChat/AugaChat/NpcDialogLarge act=1`, each with CanvasGroup m_Alpha: 1. NpcDialog.controller (guid 04810bcf53f02364b985ee48f350d591) `m_DefaultState: {fileID: 1102728390217458825}` = state `npc_dialog_show`, `m_Tag: visible`, transition -> npc_dialog_visible with hasExitTime=1 and no conditions.
- **Fix:** Set `m_IsActive: 0` on both GameObjects in AugaChat.prefab to match vanilla, or add `__instance.m_npcTextBase.SetActive(false); __instance.m_npcTextBaseLarge.SetActive(false);` to Chat_Setup.Chat_Awake_Patch.Postfix.

### [hud-bars-5] Hud_UpdateBuild_Patch's transpiler looks for a ldstr " [<color=yellow>" that 1.0 replaced with a composite format string, so the build-category counts are never recoloured

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 78)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\Hud_Setup.cs:619-633, specifically line 624 `if (instruction.opcode == OpCodes.Ldstr && instruction.OperandIs(" [<color=yellow>"))`
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud.UpdateBuild (decomp/Hud.cs:1470) `string text = $"{pieceTable.m_categoryLabels[i]} [<color=yellow>{player.GetAvailableBuildPiecesInCategory(pieceTable.m_categories[i])}</color>]";` — in 0.217 this was string concatenation: `m_buildCategoryNames[i] + " [<color=yellow>" + player.GetAvailableBuildPiecesInCategory((Piece.PieceCategory)i) + "</color>]"`
- **Trigger:** Opening the build menu with a piece table that has categories.
- **Consequence:** No exception. The transpiler matches nothing and yields the method unchanged, so the piece-count badge on each build-category tab keeps vanilla's yellow instead of Auga's BrightestGold. Purely visual inconsistency with the rest of Auga's palette.
- **Evidence:** Scanning the shipped D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\Valheim\valheim_Data\Managed\assembly_valheim.dll for UTF-16 literals: `'{0} [<color=yellow>{1}</color>]'` occurs exactly 1 time (the UpdateBuild interpolation, compiled to a single ldstr + string.Format) while `' [<color=yellow>'` occurs 2 times elsewhere in the assembly. ILSpy renders the old method with `+` concatenation and the new one with `$"..."`, confirming the IL shape changed from four-operand concat to one composite format string.
- **Fix:** Match the new literal instead: replace the needle at Hud_Setup.cs:624 with `instruction.OperandIs("{0} [<color=yellow>{1}</color>]")` and emit `$"{{0}} [<color={Auga.Colors.BrightestGold}>{{1}}</color>]"`. Keep the old branch as well so the patch survives either IL shape.

### [text-chat-5] Chat_Setup and MessageHud_Setup Awake postfixes also run for the hidden vanilla donor, adding MovableHudElement to donor objects and binding a config section named '<name>_VanillaDonor'

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 78)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\Chat_Setup.cs:24-34 (`if (__instance.m_input != null) __instance.m_input.transform.parent.gameObject.AddComponent<MovableHudElement>().Init(TextAnchor.LowerRight, 0, 67);`) and Auga\MessageHud_Setup.cs:18-28 (`__instance.m_messageCenterText.gameObject.AddComponent<MovableHudElement>().Init(TextAnchor.MiddleCenter, 0, 150);`)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Chat.Awake and MessageHud.Awake (scratchpad\decomp\Chat.cs, scratchpad\decomp\MessageHud.cs) - the Harmony prefix returns false for the original instance but the postfix still runs on it
- **Trigger:** World load, every time the replacement happens.
- **Consequence:** The postfix body executes twice: once for Auga's replacement and once for the inactive donor. For Chat that binds a bogus BepInEx config section derived from the donor's renamed GameObject (PortCarryOver.MakeDonor appends "_VanillaDonor" to the Chat_box object), polluting Auga's cfg with e.g. [Chat_box_VanillaDonor]; a stray MovableHudElement is also left on hidden donor objects.
- **Evidence:** Auga\SetupHelper.cs:69-70 `var secondaryDonor = PortCarryOver.MakeDonor(secondaryOriginal.gameObject); var primaryDonor = PortCarryOver.MakeDonor(primaryOriginal.gameObject);` and PortCarryOver.cs:23-29 `original.SetActive(false); original.name += DonorSuffix;` with `public const string DonorSuffix = "_VanillaDonor";`. MovableHudElement.Init(TextAnchor, float, float) forwards `gameObject.name` as the config section name (MovableHudElement.cs:12-21 `Init(gameObject.name, ...)` -> `Auga.instance.Config.Bind(nameOverride, $"{nameOverride}Anchor", ...)`). The Awake postfix in MessageHud_Setup already guards its controller work with `if (controller)` but the m_messageCenterText line is unguarded.
- **Fix:** Bail out of both postfixes for the donor, e.g. `if (__instance.name.EndsWith(PortCarryOver.DonorSuffix)) return;` or `if (Chat.instance != __instance) return;` / `if (MessageHud.instance != __instance) return;`.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix is right; `if (Chat.instance != __instance) return;` is the more robust of the two since it does not depend on the rename. Two caveats on the predicted symptom: for MessageHud the stray element lands on the donor's `MessageCenter` child (dumps/MessageHud_vanilla.txt:2), whose name is unchanged, so it collides with Auga's own [MessageCenter] section rather than creating a "_VanillaDonor" one; and the [Chat_box_VanillaDonor] section name holds only if m_input.transform.parent is the Chat_box root itself, which the per-component dumps cannot show.

### [auga-lib-10] ComplexTooltip drops 1.0's item subtitle and cheated-item marker because GetExtraTextFromTooltip only keeps lines after $item_weight

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 78)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/ComplexTooltip.cs:504-539 (GetExtraTextFromTooltip) and :320-346 (GenerateItemSubtext, which builds its own subtitle)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip (decomp/ItemDrop.cs:865-873)
- **Trigger:** Hover an item that has m_shared.m_subtitle set, or any item spawned with console commands (m_cheated).
- **Consequence:** The 1.0 orange subtitle line and the '$achievements_cheated_item_inventory' grey italic marker never appear in an Auga tooltip. The cheated marker matters because 1.0 uses m_cheated to gate achievements, so the player loses the only in-UI signal that an item is disqualifying.
- **Evidence:** 1.0 adds, before the weight line: `if (item.m_shared.m_subtitle.Length > 0) { m_stringBuilder.Append("\n<color=orange>" + item.m_shared.m_subtitle + "</color>"); }` and `if (item.m_cheated && !PlayerProfile.s_bypassCheatChecks) { m_stringBuilder.Append("\n<color=#808080><i>" + Localization.instance.Localize("$achievements_cheated_item_inventory") + "</i></color>"); }` (decomp/ItemDrop.cs:865-873). Both `m_subtitle` and `m_cheated` return 0 hits in decomp_old_assembly_valheim/ItemDrop.cs. Auga's extractor discards everything before the weight line — `if ((line.Contains("$item_") || line.Contains("$inventory_")) && !foundWeight) { if (line.Contains("$item_weight")) foundWeight = true; } else { if (foundWeight) { ... } }` (ComplexTooltip.cs:516-534) — and Auga's own subtitle comes only from m_itemType/m_food/m_skillType (GenerateItemSubtext, :320-346).
- **Fix:** In SetItemBaseData append `item.m_shared.m_subtitle` to the string SetSubtitle receives when it is non-empty, and in GenerateItemTextBoxes add the cheated line next to the existing `item.m_shared.m_dlc.Length > 0` centered box: `if (!upgrade && item.m_cheated && !PlayerProfile.s_bypassCheatChecks) { TextBoxAddPreprocessedLine(AddTextBox(CenteredTextBoxPrefab), item, "<color=#808080><i>$achievements_cheated_item_inventory</i></color>"); }`.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix this together with widgets-4 rather than separately — both are symptoms of GetExtraTextFromTooltip's positional heuristic, and patching the scanner once (match the known 1.0 line tokens instead of "everything after weight, after the first non-$item_ line") covers the subtitle, the cheated marker and the new equipment-modifier block in one change.

### [hud-build-9] Hud_UpdateBuild_Patch's transpiler no longer matches: 1.0 turned the category-count concatenation into an interpolated string, so the category counts stay vanilla yellow

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 75)
- **Auga:** Auga/Hud_Setup.cs:620-633 (`if (instruction.opcode == OpCodes.Ldstr && instruction.OperandIs(" [<color=yellow>")) yield return new CodeInstruction(OpCodes.Ldstr, $" [<color={Auga.Colors.BrightestGold}>");`)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud.UpdateBuild (scratchpad/decomp/Hud.cs:1470)
- **Trigger:** Every frame the build menu is open, for each category tab label.
- **Consequence:** Silent no-op - the transpiler yields the original instruction stream unchanged and Harmony reports no error - so the per-category piece counts render in stock yellow rather than Auga's BrightestGold. (Subsumed in practice by finding 1, since the tabs it would recolour are never displayed.)
- **Evidence:** 0.217.30 built the label by concatenation, which emits a standalone `ldstr " [<color=yellow>"`: `string text = m_buildCategoryNames[i] + " [<color=yellow>" + player.GetAvailableBuildPiecesInCategory((Piece.PieceCategory)i) + "</color>]";` (decomp_old_assembly_valheim/Hud.cs, hunk at Hud.diff:1574). 1.0 rewrote it as `string text = $"{pieceTable.m_categoryLabels[i]} [<color=yellow>{player.GetAvailableBuildPiecesInCategory(pieceTable.m_categories[i])}</color>]";` (decomp/Hud.cs:1470). The game is built with Unity 6000.0.75f1 (PORT_1.0.md:28), i.e. C# 9, which has no DefaultInterpolatedStringHandler, so Roslyn lowers a mixed string+int interpolation to `string.Format` with one combined literal `"{0} [<color=yellow>{1}</color>]"`. `OperandIs(" [<color=yellow>")` cannot match that.
- **Fix:** Match on the new literal instead - test for an ldstr operand that `Contains("<color=yellow>")` and do a `Replace("<color=yellow>", $"<color={Auga.Colors.BrightestGold}>")` on it - so the patch survives both the concatenation and the string.Format form.
- **Verifier's better fix:** The fix is right. Worth adding to the "moot" note: the tabs it recolours are m_pieceCategoryTabs, and only the legacy `hudroot/BuildHud/bar/SelectionWindow/Categories/Tabs/*` objects have the `Selected` child that UpdateBuild does `gameObject.transform.Find("Selected")` on (dumps/Hud_vanilla.txt:116-137); BuildUIV2's tabs have no such child. So the array still points into the window Awake deactivates, making the recolour doubly invisible.

### [pause-texts-11] Auga's Menu.UpdateNavigation replacement never sets Navigation.Mode.Explicit, so the up/down chain it builds is ignored by Unity

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 75)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\PauseMenu_Setup.cs:189-197 (`buttonList[index].navigation with { selectOnUp = ..., selectOnDown = ... }` - mode is inherited from the prefab)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Menu.UpdateNavigation() (decomp\Menu.cs:194 `navigation.mode = Navigation.Mode.Explicit;`)
- **Trigger:** Navigate the pause menu with a gamepad or arrow keys.
- **Consequence:** MenuButton.prefab ships `m_Navigation: m_Mode: 3` (Automatic), and with Automatic mode Unity computes neighbours geometrically and ignores selectOnUp/selectOnDown entirely. Auga's carefully ordered list (Settings, Compendium, Save, Logout, Exit, CloseButton) has no effect, and the wrap-around from the last entry back to the first is lost. The list also omits SkipIntro, CurrentPlayerList and the new InviteFriends entries that 1.0's UpdateNavigation includes.
- **Evidence:** AugaUnity\Assets\Prefabs\MenuButton.prefab:241-247 `m_Navigation: / m_Mode: 3 / m_SelectOnUp: {fileID: 0} / m_SelectOnDown: {fileID: 0}`. decomp\Menu.cs:175-196 sets selectOnUp/selectOnDown AND `navigation.mode = Navigation.Mode.Explicit` on every entry; Menu.diff line 186 shows 1.0 made the mode unconditional (0.217 chose Explicit-or-None at runtime). PauseMenu_Setup.cs:191-195 copies the record without touching `mode`.
- **Fix:** Add `mode = Navigation.Mode.Explicit` to the `with` expression in PauseMenu_Setup.cs:191-195, and include the SkipIntro / CurrentPlayerList / Invite buttons in buttonList when their GameObjects are active, mirroring decomp\Menu.cs:151-174.

### [text-chat-10] Chat's new 1.0 field m_doubleOpenForVirtualKeyboard is false on Auga's serialized Chat copy

- **Verdict:** cannot_verify (reviewer confidence 75)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnity\Assets\Prefabs\AugaChat.prefab lines 1409-1422 - the Chat MonoBehaviour (&2728652263308488219, m_Script fileID 306012409 guid c1b78fa918b030faf1c1f6f6164daeb2) serializes only m_chatWindow, m_output, m_input, m_search, m_maxVisibleBufferLength, m_hideDelay, m_worldTextTTL, m_worldTextBase, m_npcTextBase, m_npcTextBaseLarge, m_wasFocused - there is no m_doubleOpenForVirtualKeyboard key
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Chat.Update / the new field (scratchpad\decomp\Chat.cs, `[Tooltip("If true the player has to open chat twice to enter input mode.")] [SerializeField] protected bool m_doubleOpenForVirtualKeyboard = true;`)
- **Trigger:** Opening chat on a console platform (Application.isConsolePlatform).
- **Consequence:** The double-open virtual-keyboard behaviour 1.0 added is off on Auga's chat: `if (m_doubleOpenForVirtualKeyboard && Application.isConsolePlatform) m_input.Select(); else m_input.ActivateInputField();` always takes the ActivateInputField branch. Irrelevant on PC, hence cosmetic.
- **Evidence:** The field is new in 1.0 (Chat.diff hunk @@ -98,64 +96,67 @@ adds it); a bool that is absent from the bundle's serialized data deserializes to default(false), not to the C# initializer. PortCarryOver only fills UnityEngine.Object references and Object collections (PortCarryOver.cs:104-120), never value types.
- **Fix:** Add `m_doubleOpenForVirtualKeyboard: 1` to the Chat component in AugaChat.prefab (or set it in the Chat_Setup postfix) so the value matches the vanilla default.
- **Verifier correction:** Everything structural checks out, but the decisive claim — that a bool absent from the bundle's serialized data comes back as false rather than the C# initializer's true — is a Unity deserialization behaviour I cannot establish from the materials on disk, and it is the whole finding. Unity constructs the managed MonoBehaviour instance (running field initializers, so m_doubleOpenForVirtualKeyboard = true) before transferring the serialized stream, and a field missing from the bundle's type tree is simply not transferred, which argues the live value is more likely true — i.e. the finding may be a non-issue even on console. Deciding this needs a run, not a read.
- **Verifier's better fix:** If it is worth doing at all, set it in the Chat_Setup postfix rather than editing the prefab — that is deterministic regardless of how the bundle deserializes. But confirm the live value first (log it once on a Chat instance); the finding is cosmetic and PC-irrelevant either way, since the branch is gated on Application.isConsolePlatform.

### [harmony-15] AddKnownBiome log hook keys off BiomeSector.Biome, but 1.0 tracks known biomes by sector name, so alt-biome sectors log duplicate/base-biome entries

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 75)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\AugaLog_Hooks.cs:132-144 (Player_AddKnownBiome_Patch.Prefix)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Player.AddKnownBiome(BiomeSector biome) / Player.IsBiomeKnown(BiomeSector biome) (decomp\Player.cs:5153-5175), Player.m_knownBiome (HashSet<string>)
- **Trigger:** Discover a second, differently named sector of a biome you already know (1.0's alt-biome sectors), or spawn into Meadows.
- **Consequence:** Auga's message log writes an extra entry naming the base Heightmap.Biome (so 'Meadows' can appear more than once), and it logs the Meadows/None discovery that vanilla deliberately suppresses. No exception.
- **Evidence:** 1.0 keys discovery by name - `IsBiomeKnown => m_knownBiome.Contains(biome.GetName())`, `AddKnownBiome: string text = biome.GetName(); if (!m_knownBiome.Contains(text)) { ... if ((biome.Biome != Heightmap.Biome.Meadows && biome.Biome != Heightmap.Biome.None) || biome.AltBiomes.Count > 0) MessageHud.instance.ShowBiomeFoundMsg(text2, playStinger: true); }`. Auga's prefix does `if (biome != null && !__instance.IsBiomeKnown(biome)) AugaMessageLog.instance.AddNewBiomeLog(biome.Biome);` with no name and no Meadows/None suppression. BiomeSector exposes both `public Heightmap.Biome Biome;` and `GetName()` (decomp\BiomeSector.cs).
- **Fix:** Pass `biome.GetName()` (or the localized sector name) to a string overload of AddNewBiomeLog and mirror vanilla's `(biome.Biome != Meadows && biome.Biome != None) || biome.AltBiomes.Count > 0` condition.

### [text-chat-6] MessageHud_Setup.ShowMessage postfix dereferences AugaTopLeftMessageController without a null check and ignores 1.0's new showDespiteHiddenHUD/log parameters

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 72)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\MessageHud_Setup.cs:30-46 - `if (Hud.IsUserHidden()) return;` (line 34) then `var controller = __instance.GetComponent<AugaTopLeftMessageController>(); controller.AddMessage(text, icon, amount);` (lines 41-43)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** MessageHud.ShowMessage (scratchpad\decomp\MessageHud.cs:156-198)
- **Trigger:** Any MessageType.TopLeft message; the missing-message half triggers only for a TopLeft message sent with showDespiteHiddenHUD: true while the HUD is user-hidden.
- **Consequence:** (a) If the HudMessage/TopLeftMessage replacement ever fails to run (e.g. a mod conflict or a renamed sibling), the very first TopLeft message throws NullReferenceException inside the postfix rather than degrading. (b) 1.0's new `showDespiteHiddenHUD` contract is not honoured: vanilla enqueues the message, Auga's top-left display drops it.
- **Evidence:** 1.0 signature: `public void ShowMessage(MessageType type, string text, int amount = 0, Sprite icon = null, bool showDespiteHiddenHUD = false, bool log = true)` with the body now `m_showDespiteHiddenHUD = showDespiteHiddenHUD; if (Hud.IsUserHidden() && !showDespiteHiddenHUD) return;` - the old body was `if (!Hud.IsUserHidden()) { ... }`. Auga's postfix still uses the pre-1.0 unconditional `if (Hud.IsUserHidden()) return;` and does not declare the two new parameters, so Harmony never injects them. The sibling Awake postfix in the same file guards with `if (controller)`; this one does not.
- **Fix:** Add `bool showDespiteHiddenHUD` to the postfix parameter list and use `if (Hud.IsUserHidden() && !showDespiteHiddenHUD) return;`, and guard the controller: `if (controller == null) return;`.

### [widgets-8] ComplexTooltip's $item_value row is inverted relative to 1.0 (vanilla swapped the two arguments) and its $item_weight row drops the new per-item/total split for stackables

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 72)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib\ComplexTooltip.cs:423 `TextBoxAddPreprocessedLine(textBox, item, "$item_value", item.GetValue(), item.m_shared.m_value);` and :457 `TextBoxAddPreprocessedLine(textBox, item, "$item_weight", item.GetWeight().ToString("0.0"));`
- **Vanilla 1.0:** ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip (scratchpad\decomp\ItemDrop.cs:881-896) vs decomp_old_assembly_valheim\ItemDrop.cs:583-587
- **Trigger:** Hovering any stackable or sellable item.
- **Consequence:** Auga renders value as `total (per-unit)` where vanilla 1.0 now renders `per-unit (total $item_total)`, so a stack of 3 coins worth 10 each reads "30 (10)" under Auga and "10 (30 total)" in vanilla - the same two numbers with opposite meaning, and no `$item_total` label to disambiguate. Weight shows only the stack total; 1.0 shows non-stacked weight plus total for anything with `m_maxStackSize > 1`.
- **Evidence:** OLD: `m_stringBuilder.AppendFormat("\n$item_value: <color=orange>{0}  ({1})</color>", item.GetValue(), item.m_shared.m_value);` - Auga's argument order matched this. NEW: `m_stringBuilder.AppendFormat("\n$item_value: <color=orange>{0} ({1} $item_total)</color>", item.m_shared.m_value, item.GetValue());` - arguments swapped and a `$item_total` token added. NEW weight: `if (item.m_shared.m_maxStackSize > 1) { m_stringBuilder.AppendFormat("\n$item_weight: <color=orange>{0} ({1} $item_total)</color>", item.GetNonStackedWeight().ToString("0.0"), item.GetWeight(stackOverride).ToString("0.0")); } else { ... item.GetWeight().ToString("0.0") ... }`; OLD had only the single-value form. Auga's `GenerateParenthetical` renders `"{b} <color=#A39689>({parenthetical})</color>"`.
- **Fix:** Swap the two arguments at ComplexTooltip.cs:423 to `item.m_shared.m_value, item.GetValue()`, and at :457 branch on `item.m_shared.m_maxStackSize > 1` to pass `item.GetNonStackedWeight().ToString("0.0")` with `item.GetWeight().ToString("0.0")` as the parenthetical.

### [harmony-12] Hud.UpdateBuild transpiler's ldstr search no longer matches: 1.0 builds the category label with string.Format, not string concatenation

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 72)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\Hud_Setup.cs:619-633 (Hud_UpdateBuild_Patch.Transpiler)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Hud.UpdateBuild (decomp\Hud.cs:1470)
- **Trigger:** Open the build category tabs.
- **Consequence:** The build-category piece counts keep vanilla's yellow instead of Auga's BrightestGold. The transpiler silently matches nothing (it has no fallback and no log).
- **Evidence:** Auga looks for `instruction.opcode == OpCodes.Ldstr && instruction.OperandIs(" [<color=yellow>")`. OLD Hud.UpdateBuild: `string text = m_buildCategoryNames[i] + " [<color=yellow>" + player.GetAvailableBuildPiecesInCategory((Piece.PieceCategory)i) + "</color>]";` (rendered by ILSpy as `+`, i.e. string.Concat with a standalone ldstr " [<color=yellow>"). NEW: `string text = $"{pieceTable.m_categoryLabels[i]} [<color=yellow>{player.GetAvailableBuildPiecesInCategory(pieceTable.m_categories[i])}</color>]";` - ILSpy in this same decompile renders plain string.Concat as `+` (e.g. Hud.UpdateShownTip `ZLog.Log("tip:" + text)`, Hud.SetVisible `"$hud_hidden_messagehud_notification_gamepad " + ...`), so the interpolated form indicates a single composite format literal `"{0} [<color=yellow>{1}</color>]"`. 1.0 also deleted `m_buildCategoryNames` entirely.
- **Fix:** Match any ldstr whose operand *contains* "<color=yellow>" and string-replace inside it, rather than testing for equality with " [<color=yellow>"; log when no instruction was rewritten. (Moot while the build menu itself is the hidden legacy branch - see area_summary.)
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fine as written. Since hud-build-9 and harmony-12 are the same defect, report them once. And note the finding's own "moot" caveat is stronger than stated: m_pieceCategoryTabs points at the legacy `hudroot/BuildHud/bar/SelectionWindow/Categories/Tabs/*` objects (the only ones with the `Selected` child that decomp/Hud.cs:1468 looks up — dumps/Hud_vanilla.txt:116-137), and that window is SetActive(false) in Hud.Awake.

### [crafting-10] Auga's crafting item info shows the single-craft output, never 1.0's multi-craft amount

- **Verdict:** partly (reviewer confidence 70)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib/AugaCraftingPanel.cs:161-169 - `ItemInfo.SetItem(recipe.m_item.m_itemData, 1, variant)` / `ItemInfo.SetItem(item, quality, variant)`; ComplexTooltip.SetItem has no amount parameter (AugaUnityLib/ComplexTooltip.cs:251)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** InventoryGui.UpdateRecipe (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1510-1517)
- **Trigger:** Hold AltPlace over the crafting panel on a stacking recipe (arrows, food, nails) so 1.0 previews the x5 batch.
- **Consequence:** 1.0 computes `num3 = m_multiCraftAmount; num4 = m_selectedRecipe.Recipe.m_amount * num3;`, appends ` x{num4}` to m_recipeName and passes num4 into `ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip(..., Game.m_worldLevel, num4)` so the description states the batch size. Auga's visible panel is the ComplexTooltip, built from `recipe.m_item.m_itemData` with no amount at all, so the player gets no confirmation of how many items an AltPlace craft will produce. (The button label itself is fine - vanilla writes "$inventory_craftbutton x 5" into `m_craftButton.GetComponentInChildren<TMP_Text>()`, which is Auga's real button.)
- **Evidence:** 1.0: `int num3 = ((!flag3) ? 1 : m_multiCraftAmount); int num4 = m_selectedRecipe.Recipe.m_amount * num3; if (m_selectedRecipe.Recipe.m_amount > 1) { text = $"{text} x{num4}"; } ... ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip(m_selectedRecipe.Recipe.m_item.m_itemData, num, crafting: true, Game.m_worldLevel, num4)`. 0.217 passed no amount: `GetTooltip(..., crafting: true, Game.m_worldLevel)` (auga_audit/diffs/InventoryGui.diff). Auga: `ItemInfo.SetItem(recipe.m_item.m_itemData, 1, variant);`
- **Fix:** Pass the multi-craft amount through - add an amount parameter to AugaCraftingPanel.SetRecipe (sourced from the `amount` argument of the SetupRequirementList postfix, or recomputed as `recipe.m_amount * (multiCrafting ? InventoryGui.instance.m_multiCraftAmount : 1)`) and append it to the ComplexTooltip topic the way vanilla appends ` x{num4}` to m_recipeName.
- **Verifier correction:** Auga's ComplexTooltip genuinely never conveys the multi-craft batch (SetItem has no amount parameter - ComplexTooltip.cs:251 `SetItem(ItemDrop.ItemData item, int quality = -1, int variant = -1)` - and AugaCraftingPanel.cs:168 passes `recipe.m_item.m_itemData, 1, variant`). But the mechanism is misstated: num4 is passed as GetTooltip's new `stackOverride` parameter (decomp/ItemDrop.cs:848 `GetTooltip(ItemData item, int qualityLevel, bool crafting, float worldLevel, int stackOverride = -1, bool appending = false)`), and stackOverride is used at exactly one place - decomp/ItemDrop.cs:890, the `$item_weight: {0} ({1} $item_total)` line. The description does not 'state the batch size'. The visible ` x{num4}` goes into m_recipeName (decomp/InventoryGui.cs:1512-1516) and only when `m_selectedRecipe.Recipe.m_amount > 1`; for a recipe with m_amount == 1 an AltPlace x5 craft produces no ' x5' in the name at all. So what Auga loses is the ' x{num4}' name suffix and the batch-scaled $item_total weight, not a batch statement in the description.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Append the amount to the ComplexTooltip topic the way vanilla appends it to m_recipeName - i.e. only when `recipe.m_amount > 1`, using `recipe.m_amount * (multiCrafting ? InventoryGui.instance.m_multiCraftAmount : 1)` - and separately pass that total into the weight figure, since ComplexTooltip.cs:457 emits `item.GetWeight()` with no stackOverride and so always shows the single-unit total.

### [pause-texts-12] TabHandler moved Init from Awake to Start and added m_activeTabEverSet, so the compendium's ShowCompendium call silently cancels the initial tab selection

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 70)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnityLib\AugaCompendiumController.cs:137-138 (`gameObject.SetActive(true); TabController.SetActiveTab(0);`); the TabHandler is Compendium.prefab &4197022688427604870 (line 287, `m_Script: {fileID: -259742986, guid: c1b78fa918b030faf1c1f6f6164daeb2}`)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** TabHandler.Start()/Init()/SetActiveTab(int,bool,bool) (decomp\TabHandler.cs:53-56, :108-111, :228-235)
- **Trigger:** First time the compendium is opened after the pause menu is created (the Compendium instance ships inactive).
- **Consequence:** `SetActive(true)` no longer triggers Init (1.0 moved it from Awake to Start, which is deferred). `SetActiveTab(0)` then sets `m_activeTabEverSet = true` and immediately returns because `!forceSelect && m_selected == 0`. When Start->Init finally runs, `if (!m_activeTabEverSet && num >= 0)` is false, so the default tab is never applied: all three tab buttons keep `interactable = true` and their 'Selected' highlight children keep their raw prefab state. It self-corrects only after the player clicks a tab other than the first.
- **Evidence:** TabHandler.diff lines 39-45 change `-	private void Awake()` to `+	private void Start()`; line 26 adds `+	private bool m_activeTabEverSet;`; line 113 changes `-		if (num >= 0)` to `+		if (!m_activeTabEverSet && num >= 0)`; line 245 adds `+		m_activeTabEverSet = true;` as the first statement of SetActiveTab, before the `if (!forceSelect && m_selected == index) return;` guard (decomp\TabHandler.cs:230-234). AugaMenu.prefab lines 1823-1826 override the Compendium instance root to `m_IsActive: 0`, so Awake/Start are both deferred to the first ShowCompendium. Compendium.prefab's three tabs are Tutorials (m_default: 1), Trophies, Lore.
- **Fix:** Call `TabController.SetActiveTab(0, forceSelect: true)` in AugaCompendiumController.ShowCompendium, or call `TabController.Init(forceSelect: true)` before SetActiveTab so Init's default-tab pass is not suppressed.

### [widgets-7] ButtonSfx.OnSelect was an empty method in 0.217 and now plays m_selectSfxPrefab, which 33 of Auga's 51 ButtonSfx components have assigned

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 66)
- **Auga:** 33 non-null `m_selectSfxPrefab` entries across AugaUnity\Assets\Prefabs (e.g. AugaMenu.prefab:383, BuildHud.prefab:145/948/1873/2298, AugaSettings.prefab:180/1830/4310/5009), all pointing at guid `515393240de246c44a837291defbe135`
- **Vanilla 1.0:** ButtonSfx.OnSelect / ButtonSfx.PlaySfx (scratchpad\decomp_assembly_guiutils\ButtonSfx.cs), vs decomp_old_assembly_guiutils\ButtonSfx.cs:55-58
- **Trigger:** Any time the EventSystem selects an Auga button - clicking it, gamepad navigation, or UIGroupHandler auto-selecting its `m_defaultElement`.
- **Consequence:** An extra UI sound on every selection, and a doubled sound on a mouse click (select fires on pointer-down, click on pointer-up, which is normally more than the 2-frame `SfxTimer` window). Auga's screens were authored when the field was dead, so the volume/frequency was never tuned.
- **Evidence:** OLD decomp_old_assembly_guiutils\ButtonSfx.cs:55-58 is literally `public void OnSelect(BaseEventData eventData) { }`. NEW: `public void OnSelect(BaseEventData eventData) { PlaySfx(m_selectSfxPrefab, m_selectSfxPrefabVibrationOnly); }` with `private void PlaySfx(GameObject soundPrefab, GameObject vibrationPrefab) { if ((bool)soundPrefab && m_sfxTimer.HasTimedOut()) { Object.Instantiate(soundPrefab); m_sfxTimer.Reset(); } ... }` and `private static SfxTimer m_sfxTimer` (SfxTimer.HasTimedOut = `Time.frameCount - m_lastTriggerFrame > 2`). Counts: `grep -rn m_selectSfxPrefab --include=*.prefab` = 52 lines, of which 33 are not `{fileID: 0}`.
- **Fix:** Decide per screen: either clear `m_selectSfxPrefab` on Auga's ButtonSfx components at setup time (`foreach (var sfx in root.GetComponentsInChildren<ButtonSfx>(true)) sfx.m_selectSfxPrefab = null;`) or point it at a quieter dedicated select sound, matching whatever the vanilla 1.0 prefabs use.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix is right. Targeting note for the per-screen decision: of the 33, 6 are on BuildHud.prefab (which only ever lives under the inactive dummy) and 4 on AugaSettings.prefab (excluded from the build), while the ones that actually fire in play come from the shared base prefabs — MenuButton, ButtonSmall/Medium/Fancy/Diamond, ButtonToggle, IncrementButton, TabButton_Base — so clearing those eight covers nearly all of it.

### [pause-texts-14] TabHandler.m_tabKeyInput is new in 1.0 and defaults to true, so the Tab key now cycles the compendium's tabs although Auga's prefab has m_gamepadInput off

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 65)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\AugaUnity\Assets\Prefabs\Compendium.prefab:287-305 (TabHandler &4197022688427604870 serializes only m_gamepadInput: 0, m_gamepadNavigateLeft, m_gamepadNavigateRight, m_tabs, m_setActiveTabEffects)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** TabHandler.Update() (decomp\TabHandler.cs:154-157 `if (m_tabKeyInput && ZInput.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Tab)) num = 1;`)
- **Trigger:** Press Tab while the compendium is open.
- **Consequence:** In 0.217 the Tab-key branch lived inside `if (m_gamepadInput && ...)`, so with m_gamepadInput: 0 Tab did nothing in the compendium. In 1.0 the check is top-level and gated only by the new `m_tabKeyInput`, which is absent from Auga's serialized data and therefore keeps its C# initializer value `true`. Tab now silently cycles compendium tabs, which can fight with any other Tab handling the player expects.
- **Evidence:** TabHandler.diff line 12 adds `+	public bool m_tabKeyInput = true;`; lines 160-166 show 0.217 had `if (ZInput.GetKeyDown((KeyCode)9))` nested inside the `if (m_gamepadInput ...)` block, while lines 167-170 show 1.0 hoisted it to `if (m_tabKeyInput && ZInput.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Tab))` outside that block. auga_field_diff.csv row "TabHandler": New = `m_cycling, m_tabKeyInput, m_keybaordInput, m_keyboardNavigateLeft, m_keyboardNavigateRight, m_blockingElements`. Compendium.prefab's TabHandler YAML (lines 299-301) has no m_tabKeyInput key.
- **Fix:** Set `m_tabKeyInput: 0` on Compendium.prefab's TabHandler (and on the other four Auga TabHandler instances), or assign it at runtime after the bundle loads, to preserve the pre-1.0 behaviour.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix is right. Minor: there are two other Auga TabHandler instances, not four — AugaSettings.prefab:6291 and MainMenu.prefab:5930 — and both of those screens are currently disabled in the port (Settings_Setup.cs excluded from the build, MainMenu_Setup's swap block commented out), so Compendium.prefab is the only one that matters today.

### [text-chat-9] IndirectTwoObjectReplace captures both sibling indices before moving both donors to last, so the secondary object lands one slot late when it was originally after the primary

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 60)
- **Auga:** C:\Users\user_\Documents\GitHub\Valheim_Creative\Auga\Auga\SetupHelper.cs:62-84 - `var secondarySiblingIndex = secondaryOriginal.GetSiblingIndex(); var primarySiblingIndex = primaryOriginal.GetSiblingIndex();` are both read before `PortCarryOver.MakeDonor(secondaryOriginal...)` and `PortCarryOver.MakeDonor(primaryOriginal...)`, each of which calls `transform.SetAsLastSibling()`
- **Vanilla 1.0:** Chat.Awake and MessageHud.Awake call sites - the replaced pairs are IngameGui/Chat + IngameGui/Chat_box (Auga\Chat_Setup.cs:21) and IngameGui/HudMessage + IngameGui/TopLeftMessage (Auga\MessageHud_Setup.cs:15)
- **Trigger:** World load, only when the secondary object (Chat_box / TopLeftMessage) is a later sibling of IngameGui than the primary (Chat / HudMessage).
- **Consequence:** The replacement chat window / top-left message container is inserted one slot further back in IngameGui's child order than the vanilla object it replaces, changing uGUI draw order against its immediate neighbours. DirectObjectReplace (single donor) does not have this problem.
- **Evidence:** With children [... secondary@b ... primary@c ...] and b > c: MakeDonor(secondary) moves b to the end (indices > b shift down by 1), MakeDonor(primary) moves c to the end (indices > c shift down by 1), so the live element that was at b now sits at b-1; `secondary.SetSiblingIndex(b)` therefore inserts one position past its original slot. For b < c the arithmetic works out correctly, which is why the bug is order-dependent and silent.
- **Fix:** Re-read the indices after the donors are moved, or move a donor only immediately before inserting its replacement: `var secondaryDonor = MakeDonor(...); var secondarySiblingIndex = ...` per pair, or capture both, insert the replacements first and move the donors last.
- **Verifier's better fix:** Fix is right. Whether it actually bites is the one thing I could not check: none of the dumps show IngameGui's own child order (PortHierarchyDump prints only the component's own subtree), so I cannot tell whether Chat_box/TopLeftMessage precede or follow Chat/HudMessage. Re-reading the indices after the donors move is correct regardless of order and costs nothing.

### [widgets-9] GetBoundKeyString now returns a TMP <sprite=...> tag for gamepad bindings, but Auga's binding widgets write it into UnityEngine.UI.Text, and their legacy KeyCode name fixups no longer match 1.0's display strings

- **Verdict:** confirmed (reviewer confidence 60)
- **Auga:** AugaUnityLib\KeyBindDisplay.cs:10-31 (`[RequireComponent(typeof(Text))] ... _text = GetComponent<Text>(); ... var key = Localization.instance.GetBoundKeyString(ZInputId); ... _text.text = _key;`) and AugaUnityLib\AugaBindingDisplay.cs:10-12/39/51-79/108-120 (`public Text KeybindText; public Text LongKeybindText;` fed from `Localization.instance.GetBoundKeyString(keyName)`, with `case "Equals": ... case "BackQuote": ...`, `StartsWith("Keypad")`, `StartsWith("Alpha")`)
- **Vanilla 1.0:** ZInput.GetBoundKeyString (scratchpad\decomp_assembly_utils\ZInput.cs:2142-2217), s_keyLocalizationMap (ZInput.cs:620-638)
- **Trigger:** Any Auga binding widget asked for a gamepad-source button (reachable once the `_cachedKeyNames` loop in Hud_Setup.cs is fixed, since it indexes every entry of `m_buttons` including the Joy* defs), and any keyboard key whose display string Auga tries to prettify.
- **Consequence:** A gamepad binding renders as the literal text `<sprite="xbox" name="button_a">` - `UnityEngine.UI.Text` rich text supports b/i/color/size/material/quad only, not TMP sprite tags. For keyboard keys, none of the `Equals`/`BackQuote`/`Keypad*`/`Alpha*` fixups ever fire because 1.0 returns `InputBinding.ToDisplayString(...)` ("Numpad +", "=", "5") instead of legacy KeyCode names, so those `Replace` calls are dead code.
- **Evidence:** ZInput.cs:2216 `return "<sprite=\"" + text3 + "\" name=\"" + s_gamepadSpriteMap[key][text3] + "\">";` for `value.Source == InputSource.Gamepad`; for keyboard/mouse: `if (!s_keyLocalizationMap.TryGetValue(key, out var value2)) { return inputBinding.ToDisplayString(InputBinding.DisplayStringOptions.DontIncludeInteractions, Keyboard.current); } return value2;` with `s_keyLocalizationMap` holding only comma, period, leftButton, rightButton, middleButton, space, shift/leftShift/rightShift, alt/leftAlt/rightAlt, ctrl/leftCtrl/rightCtrl, enter. The old implementation (decomp_old_assembly_utils\ZInput.cs:1027ff) returned literal KeyCode-derived names via a `switch ((int)key)` over `value.m_key`, which is exactly what AugaBindingDisplay's string cases were written against. AugaBindingDisplay.cs:85-106 was already partly ported (`GetBoundControl` reads `binding.effectivePath`), but SetText at :108-120 still assigns straight into `Text`.
- **Fix:** Have the binding widgets fall back to a TMP_Text when the string contains `<sprite`, or convert the sprite tag to Auga's own glyph sprites: expose the raw `ButtonDef.ButtonAction.bindings[0].effectivePath`, map the `<Gamepad>/...` control to an Auga icon, and only use `GetBoundKeyString` for keyboard/mouse defs. Delete the dead `Equals`/`BackQuote`/`Keypad`/`Alpha` string fixups in AugaBindingDisplay.SetBinding since `GetBoundControl` already covers those via the path.
